## Intent and non-goals

Implement Q.1-Q.8 from the owner's roadmap: one useful navigation path from organization/objective to project, issue, execution and evidence; decision-focused daily work; explicit delivery/follow-up facts. Reuse the modular monolith and current operating views, not an enterprise admin suite.

No new task manager, lifecycle, permission path, scheduler, runtime, pricing system or primary persisted delivery entity. No automatic Harness apply, preview execution, client message, commitment completion, publication, deployment, retry/resume, maintenance cutover, backup/restore, normal-state migration or sibling mutation. No project completion percentage inferred from issue counts.

| Existing source | Observed capability | Q change proposed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `OrganizationView.tsx`, `organization-service.ts`, `lib/organization.ts` | Company editing, objective cards and canonical graph links; no cross-domain objective drill-down | Add an operating overview and an objective detail panel; retain existing protected editors |

The private roadmap remains input only and is excluded from commits/push. No current remote CI state or paid/live-runtime qualification is inferred from old local checkpoints.

## Architecture and read contract

1. Add pure `lib/operating-views.ts` projections and strict derived-response validation. Input is a bounded bag of already validated source facts with origin, observation time, coverage and availability. No filesystem/process/network calls in the projector.
2. Add `dashboard/src/server/operating-view-service.ts` with injected contained/no-migration readers and clock. Reuse existing read contracts and cached/saved observations; never call refresh, migrate, reconcile, dispatch or a provider as a side effect of a Q GET. Missing adapter observations remain unavailable.
3. Wire additive loopback GETs under `/api/operating/overview`, `/api/operating/objectives/:id`, `/api/operating/projects/:id` and `/api/operating/issues/:id`. Validate IDs and closed query fields; use `Cache-Control: no-store`. Existing issue/task/evidence detail routes remain authoritative and are opened explicitly, not copied wholesale into a global response.
4. Extend the current dashboard API client and views. Keep UI, application composition, policy, adapters and state ownership distinct. No derived status or action descriptor is execution authority.

`OperatingView` is an independently versioned **derived API response** (`schema_version: 1`), not a persisted aggregate or a state migration. Each section carries `available`, `absent`, `unavailable` or `not-enabled`, a stable reason code, nullable source observation time and explicit completeness. Empty optional data is distinct from a failed read. Fetch time never substitutes for evidence freshness.

Proposed bounds: at most 50 visible rows per section/page, validated page size 1..50 and safe cursors; existing tighter source limits win. Totals are exact only for fully validated covered input, otherwise null/partial. Never hide malformed records by using a filtered list as authoritative inventory. Fail a required source closed for its section while retaining independent sections. Do not recursively load all lineage/history for an overview.

Return allowlisted IDs, public labels, states, bounded reasons, evidence references and timestamps, not client confidential notes, raw task instructions/results/logs, diffs, source-document bodies, credentials or absolute paths. Client-feedback text stays in explicitly opened issue detail and is not copied to Today recommendations, quick-task prefills or model context. Error responses are normalized, never raw exceptions.

## Views and navigation

### Q.1 SURTEC overview

Extend the existing SURTEC view, preserving company editing. Show mission/principles, active objectives, project kind/lifecycle distribution, critical signals, at-risk commitments, issues/executions, human decisions, budget diagnostics, saved Harness health and current Work Session. Every card shows its observation/freshness and opens the existing relevant detail. A degraded optional source does not turn the whole app into an outage.

P budget diagnostics are labeled **not enabled / preview only** while cutover is disabled. Unknown balances are not zero, a configured policy is not enforced production authority and no new admission is enabled here. Pending continuity is shown as deferred when relevant, with no enabled backup/restore controls.

### Q.2 Objective detail

Add a compact `ObjectiveDetailPanel`: outcome/criteria, parent ancestry, canonical linked projects, commitments, active/blocked issues, recent executions/evidence, explained health, target/review date and next action. Deduplicate graph contributions; do not assign an issue to every objective merely because its project has multiple links. Missing/invalid links are gaps, not fabricated ancestry or a healthy objective. Preserve the current depth/cycle checks.

### Q.3 Issue and decision detail

Reorganize the existing Issues view into outcome/ancestry, scope/classification, current decision, workflow/attempts/handoffs, exact candidate/testing/review and audit/session evidence. Display work scale, scope revision, effective testing/review requirements, candidate validity and bounded correction consumption. Use existing service facts, not inferred authority from labels or titles. Display only currently valid human actions and call their existing protected endpoints with current revisions/previews.

The six primary operating labels are Working, Checking, Needs Decision, Ready for Review, Done and Blocked (Spanish UI equivalents). This is a presentation projection, not a new state machine. Preserve Proposed/Authorized/Cancelled as explicit secondary states where applicable; never coerce them into Done. A finished task is not a done issue. Done requires the existing authoritative issue acceptance; technical and delivery facts stay separate.

Every Needs Decision card identifies the actual known trigger and next safe action: scope/policy drift, conflicting/missing evidence, exhausted applicable limits, correction refusal, required Harness gap or separately authorized external action. If the source cannot classify the trigger, show that specific evidence gap, not an invented reason. Conflicting source states remain a conflict; the UI must not choose a permission-winning state.

### Q.4 Project Harness

Extend `ProjectHarnessPanel`, not another inspector. Show configured source/canonical version, detected version, capability coverage, routing/Doctor/drift, observation time and last **recorded** validation result. A script or matching file hash is not a successful done gate, ownership or permission. Remediation preview lists findings, affected relative entries, proposed manual review and unknown ownership; never apply/repair/overwrite or execute a registered command. Existing explicit protected refresh remains separate from opening the view.

### Q.5 Today lineage

For every primary recommendation expose objective (or intentional personal unalignment), project, issue/signal, suggested action and permitted registered role/workflow when known. Keep unavailable links and source times visible. Opening a project uses in-app detail; opening an editor is explicitly labeled. Any prepared task is only a prefill: normal preview, consent, scope, policy and approval remain mandatory. Missing optional objective/client/Harness information alone does not block a personal experiment.

### Q.7 Project daily loop

Add a small `ProjectReadinessPanel` to current project detail: repository/configuration and integration evidence, applicable verification, saved Harness observation, blocker and next permitted navigation. The action sequence is select/register -> inspect readiness -> choose/propose issue -> preview -> authorize through existing flow -> inspect checks/review -> record outcome -> pause/continue session. No duplicate issue editor, second task queue or shortcut around preview. Readiness means ready to inspect/preview, not authorized to execute.

### Q.8 Delivery and follow-up

Project these facts independently: exact technical acceptance, human-reviewed delivery readiness, a timestamped environment observation, operator-recorded handoff/client response, and follow-up status. An agent completion, green build, healthy URL or commitment state cannot fill another fact. A personal experiment can finish with a verified result and next action, without client/release ceremony.

Existing commitments lack exact candidate/evidence and delivery provenance fields. Existing issue evidence already owns candidate/scope references, timestamps and audit lineage. **Proposed smallest extension:** optional `delivery_observation` on `IssueEvidenceReference`, with nested `schema_version: 1`, kind (`readiness-reviewed`, `environment-observed`, `handoff-recorded`, `client-response-recorded`, `follow-up-recorded`), `origin: operator-entered`, nullable same-project commitment ID, explicit occurred-at time, bounded summary/source reference and next action. The evidence's existing created-at is server-record time; neither time backfills historical facts. Absence remains unsupported/unrecorded, not inferred success. Null/malformed/future facets fail closed without legacy fallback or byte repair.

Use existing `work-product`/`provider` informational evidence semantics and same issue aggregate/lock; manual facts never masquerade as passed verification/review. No new primary entity/store or commitment-state migration is proposed. Existing issue/candidate/commitment links must match project, revision and scope; missing/stale relations are gaps/refusals. No cross-store transaction or automatic commitment update is claimed.

Propose a protected `POST /api/work-issues/:id/delivery-observations` through the current issue service, loopback/origin/session checks and expected issue revision. Re-read and validate under the existing issue writer; append evidence and an audit event without changing lifecycle, authorized scope, task ownership, grants, correction allowance or client commitment. A repeated stale request is rejected without another append. Proposed per-input limits: summary 500 characters, source reference 300, next action 500; at most 64 manual facts per issue under tighter existing aggregate limits, refusing overflow without eviction. References are validated data, never fetched, executed or treated as filesystem paths. Existing accepted/review evidence cannot be edited into approval via this endpoint.

Provide `DeliveryReadinessPanel` in issue/project/commitment context, with explicit manual provenance and confirmation before recording. No send, publish, deploy or automatic client acceptance action. Follow-up defects use ordinary scoped issues; recording a follow-up does not close those issues. Client feedback is confidential local context, excluded from summaries/prefills/model context unless existing explicit sharing policy permits it.

T009 implementation binding: the closed request carries explicit project, issue revision, scope revision, nullable candidate ID/revision/fingerprint triple, the closed observation and `confirm: true`. An all-null candidate triple records an unbound observation, never silently selects a candidate. A supplied candidate must be the latest non-invalidated candidate with exact same-project/scope/revision/fingerprint bindings when recording; historical reads retain those bindings after later invalidation/scope changes. A commitment is checked against the issue's links and a strict stable local snapshot without writing that store or claiming cross-store atomicity. References are inert opaque identifiers or simple HTTP(S) URLs without userinfo, query, fragment, escapes or filesystem syntax; no network probe is performed.

New records are informational work products with null task/execution identities and no testing receipt fingerprint. Private text exists only in the nested observation, not outer labels/references or audit payloads. Observed-at cannot exceed server-recorded-at. Recording refuses a queued/running linked task or an owner workflow other than completed/cancelled, because an unrelated revision bump would invalidate existing execution progress guards. This is refusal at the new endpoint, not a change to those guards. Later previews/approvals must use the new issue revision normally. Delivery presentation remains T010.

## Ordered implementation plan

The approved task file defines twelve slices: pure contract/projection; read-only service/API; SURTEC/navigation; objective detail; issue decisions; Harness; Today lineage; project daily loop; manual evidence extension; delivery UI; focused real browser acceptance; final documentation/gates. T001-T011 are implemented under their scoped evidence; T012 remains prospective. No parallel/delegation permission is implied.

Tests precede/accompany behavior. Each slice receives focused tests for its changed contract and typecheck; UI slices get relevant component/build/browser checks in proportion to risk. Do not run a full profiling campaign after each task. No new observer layer, deadline increase, relaxed assertion or hidden suite filter is part of Q.

## Acceptance and stopping rule

The proposed Harness checks are the unchanged exact-catalog commands `pnpm test`, `pnpm schemas:check`, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build`. They prove their own layers only. Future guarded execution requires actual Q approval and explicit run authority; no new catalog entry or timeout is proposed.

Before final acceptance, obtain fresh ordinary CI/audit and real isolated browser evidence, including the focused Q smoke and existing local-app compatibility smoke. Run each final campaign once at its authorized boundary, not repeatedly while drafting. If a check fails or is unknown, record the concrete result and stop that acceptance sequence; propose a bounded functional correction only if needed. No automatic profiler expansion or acceptance waiver. A known baseline failure remains visible and prevents a green whole-repository claim even if focused Q checks pass.

| Scenario | Required observable evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Healthy company/objective/project/issue | In-app navigation reaches exact issue/task/candidate evidence without losing selection; source provenance visible |
| Empty/personal project | No required client/objective ceremony, no fabricated totals or success, explicit next permitted action |
| Degraded/malformed/stale optional source | Independent panels survive; source unavailable/stale and preserved bytes; GETs create/migrate/refresh nothing |
| Blocked/Needs Decision/budget-limited | Exact known reason or explicit gap; no unauthorized task, grant, retry or paid/provider effect |
| Missing/invalid Harness | Recorded validation distinct from presence; remediation is preview only and project bytes unchanged |
| Client delivery | Accepted candidate, manual readiness, environment, handoff/response and open follow-up remain separate; no automatic fulfillment |
| Manual evidence refusal | Missing session, stale revision, mismatched project/candidate, future facet, invalid reference and overflow reject without byte changes |
| Reload/API restart/mobile | Links and saved facts survive; token rotates, stale mutation fails; readable controls, bounded buttons, keyboard focus and no horizontal overflow |
| Privacy/cleanup | No private notes/feedback in overview/prefill/context/logs; zero real model/provider/publication effects; API/browser/Vite closed and only owned temporary workspace removed |

Browser acceptance uses actual createApp/Vite/Playwright, fictional repositories/state and fake runtime/providers, never a running user instance. Follow the existing isolated fixture pattern without importing a bootstrap that starts an unwanted second server. Add `pnpm smoke:operating-views:playwright` as a reviewed package script only during its task; no new dependency is assumed. Manual review is limited to genuinely subjective UX/preferences after automated checks, not delegated repeatable acceptance.

Stop after the approved Q slice/phase boundary. R provisioning, S/T evaluation/benchmarks, external messages, paid smokes, publication, push/merge and live-project pilots remain out of scope. Approval and scoped checks do not establish a first-usable-platform release or acceptance of later tasks.

## T002 delivery checkpoint - 2026-10-16

The owner's explicit continuation authorized T002 after T001 delivery. Work stayed on `codex/phase-q-operating-views`, same HEAD, with no staged change or commit. Product edits are exactly `dashboard/src/server/operating-view-service.ts`, its service/API test files, `dashboard/src/server/index.ts`, `dashboard/src/server/index.test.ts` and `dashboard/src/ui/api.ts`; only Q approval/evidence artifacts accompany them. No T001 schema/model change or deferred P repair/activation was required.

The service captures a configured state/projects root and injected clock without startup I/O. Each request shares validated aggregates only within that request, and isolates source faults. It uses `readProjectProfilesSnapshot`, never a migration-bearing profile list, and strict task-directory reads instead of the legacy skip-and-warn list. Existing reader validation/cross-record invariants remain authoritative. Paths are checked for symlink/junction redirection, file type and hard links, with size/count caps and before/after identity checks. These are contained cooperating-writer reads, not a cross-store transaction or hostile same-user filesystem race guarantee. No lock, directory creation, backup, migration, repair, Git status command or runtime effect is part of these reads.

Project inventory is a contained depth-one repository-presence read, overlaid with validated v2 profiles and canonical organization links. Existing personal/active defaults for unprofiled repositories are explicitly partial, not evidence of configuration or readiness. V1 profile bytes remain untouched and their section unavailable. Missing optional stores remain absent; malformed/future stores remain unavailable. Empty validated collections can have exact zero totals, but missing or partial evidence cannot. Raw task instructions/results/logs, client confidential fields, session notes/results, Harness findings/commands and source exceptions never reach the DTO. Evidence IDs are a bounded preview; existing explicit detail routes remain authoritative for full history and acceptance.

Saved timestamp instants are canonicalized conservatively; date-only review fields remain `reviewed_on`, never invented midnight observations. Source observation time is not request generation time. Candidate validity defaults unknown unless stored invalidation/scope mismatch proves staleness; current Git validity and verification are not inferred. V2 task/issue linkage uses v2 admission and exact task/project/index membership, not legacy metadata. Stored Harness validation remains separate from healthy presence. Decision codes come only from explicit scope proposals/unresolved indexed blockers, otherwise the projector records an evidence gap.

The production signal service has no saved feed adapter: the Q section is explicitly unavailable rather than invoking its provider-reading `read()` method. Trusted injected observation readers can supply existing normalized cache/saved facts. Budgets remain not-enabled diagnostics and continuity deferred; no routine/budget reconciliation or admission is called.

The four routes are `/api/operating/overview`, `/api/operating/objectives/:id`, `/api/operating/projects/:id` and `/api/operating/issues/:id`. Query fields are closed: optional `page_size` 1..50 and one `cursor_<section>` per known section, with duplicate/unsafe/unknown fields refused. No `refresh`, root, scope override or execution flag is accepted. No-store applies before existing loopback/origin/fetch-site/session security, including denials and unsupported methods. GET responses validate schema, view/focus and page bounds. A provably absent focus returns normalized 404; unavailable focus remains explicit in a validated degraded response, not fabricated not-found. Unexpected service/response faults return a fixed 503, never raw exceptions. There is no operating mutation route.

Client helpers `getOperatingOverview`, `getOperatingObjective`, `getOperatingProject` and `getOperatingIssue` use GET/no-store, no session fetch or action endpoint. They reject unsupported response/focus/page bindings and private error bodies. The same T001 validator is imported only when a helper is used; no validator or bundle warning threshold was weakened.

## T003 delivery checkpoint - 2026-10-16

The owner explicitly requested T003 after T002. Product edits are exactly `dashboard/src/ui/views/OrganizationView.tsx`, new `OrganizationView.test.tsx`, `dashboard/src/ui/App.tsx`, `App.test.tsx` and `dashboard/src/ui/styles/dashboard.css`. Only Q approval/evidence artifacts accompany them. Branch remains `codex/phase-q-operating-views`, HEAD `53ed01fd23bf62f1f08381b48f74b70595ef33f5`, empty index; existing P/user changes and private prompts remain preserved.

SURTEC consumes `getOperatingOverview` separately from the existing protected organization editor read. Eleven sections show bounded public facts, availability/reason, provenance, source observation/expiry, freshness, coverage and unknown totals. Generation time never substitutes for source observation. Each page renders at most six rows per section, replacing rather than accumulating earlier pages; cursors stay scoped per section and reset on explicit read refresh. Project kind/lifecycle distribution describes only the visible page, not the whole portfolio. Source errors retain a clearly unconfirmed previous read, never a fabricated empty inventory.

The DTO does not expose signal severity or classified commitment risk. The UI explicitly says these are unclassified, rather than guessing critical/at-risk counts. Unavailable signals do not become zero alerts or healthy objectives. Objective editor labels distinguish structural/review gaps from unconfirmed health; fuller explained health stays T004. Current sessions are saved state, not proof of live activity. Recorded Harness validation is distinct from presence; finished task results are distinct from accepted issues. Budget diagnostics remain not enabled, balances unknown and continuity deferred, with no activation controls.

Navigation is a discriminated typed callback, never an execution action. Existing project/task/workflow destinations retain exact IDs; project-to-task return preserves its project, and sidebar changes clear incompatible selections, workflow focus and prefills. Objective links focus the exact existing objective card or explain that it is unavailable; no new detail panel is implemented before T004. Generic issues carry their exact ID/project reference plus a native anchor into the existing Issues list, without fabricating a workflow or claiming loaded/validated issue detail. Issue redesign remains T005. Commitments/sessions open their existing views, not a nonexistent exact record editor. Existing explicitly requested VS Code launch remains unchanged and separately tested.

Company/objective/link editors retain their existing protected helpers. Operating refreshes preserve unsaved drafts. A save during a pending projection read queues a fresh first-page read; old/unmounted responses are discarded. Editor rereads are generation-bound so an older objective reread cannot overwrite a later company/link snapshot. Successful save evidence and reread failures are separate, preventing a misleading invitation to repeat a successful write. Fixed public errors exclude raw exceptions.

## T004 delivery checkpoint - 2026-10-16

Actual owner continuation authorized T004 after T003. Product paths are the new `dashboard/src/ui/components/ObjectiveDetailPanel.tsx` and its test, plus `dashboard/src/ui/views/OrganizationView.tsx`, its test, `dashboard/src/ui/App.tsx` and its test. Only the Q spec/task/design approval/evidence artifacts accompany those six paths. Work remains on `codex/phase-q-operating-views`, unchanged HEAD `53ed01fd23bf62f1f08381b48f74b70595ef33f5`, empty index; dirty P work and private inputs are preserved.

The new panel reads only `getOperatingObjective(id, query)`, with eight rows per section/page and no global inventory, provider refresh or editor-source fallback. Outcome/criteria, target/review dates, bounded canonical ancestry/gaps, contributing projects, issues, workflows, commitments, executions/evidence, saved Harness/session observations and disabled budget diagnostics retain independent availability/reason, provenance, observation/expiry, freshness, coverage and unknown totals. Closing returns focus to the exact existing card; independent source failures do not disable the protected editors or another operating read.

An objective page contains ancestors, focus and descendants, sorted by ID. If the focus is not on the current page, its facts remain explicitly unconfirmed on that page instead of borrowing another row or silently fetching an entire graph. Pagination replaces rows; request generations bind focus and discard superseded/unmounted responses. A failed reread can retain only same-focus evidence, explicitly marked unconfirmed. Canonical projects are deduplicated, and issues never inherit every secondary objective. Existing commitment adapters use the project's primary objective; the panel explains this rather than filling gaps from unrelated global commitments.

The DTO has no per-execution timestamps, so recency remains unavailable: the UI presents related executions ordered by ID, never labels them most recent. Review dates remain literal dates and do not backfill observation instants. Health is a deterministic explanation of visible evidence/criteria/review/coverage gaps, not a percentage, global ranking, lifecycle, grant or guarantee. Visible issue/workflow/task decisions can show attention even when an issue is still working; a historical finished/needs-review task can remain visible after later acceptance without becoming a current blocker. Candidate validity may stay unknown, and completed tasks/workflows never imply accepted issues or delivery. A next step only navigates to a visible reference, without execution or an inferred global priority. P remains disabled/deferred.

Organization edits retain their existing protected helpers. Detail open/close and independent read refreshes preserve unsaved company/objective/link drafts. Successful company/objective/link saves invalidate the mounted detail without additional mutations; stale reads cannot restore another objective's data. Typed return context now preserves objective -> project -> task -> project -> objective and objective -> issue/workflow -> task -> immediate issue/workflow -> objective. Sidebar navigation clears it; selecting/returning carries only an ID, never instructions, client context, consent or permission. Existing explicit editor launch is unchanged. The new long detail-button label wraps within its card using a local inline presentation rule; the stylesheet remains byte-identical.

## T005 implementation and acceptance checkpoint - 2026-10-16

The owner's subsequent request authorized T005 only. Modified product files are `dashboard/src/ui/views/IssuesView.tsx`, its test, `dashboard/src/ui/components/WorkflowPanel.tsx`, its test, `dashboard/src/ui/views/TaskDetailView.tsx` and its test. The only other edits are this design checkpoint, `.agents/specs/operating-views.spec.yaml` continuation metadata and `.agents/specs/tasks/phase-q-operating-views.tasks.md` approval/evidence/completion. CSS, App, API/client/model/schema/state/runtime, dependencies, Harness scripts/pins and deferred P code remain unchanged relative to the selected before-slice observations.

Issues now present recorded outcome/ancestry/assignment, scope/classification and source timestamps, decision reason/next action, workflow/attempt/session references, candidate/testing/review and audit-coverage gaps. Working/Checking/Needs Decision/Ready for Review/Done/Blocked are labels only; proposed/authorized/cancelled retain their explicit secondary meanings. A completed task/workflow is not an accepted issue, and acceptance is not delivery. Known scope proposals and exact pending workflow decision kinds are identified; recorded block reasons are not parsed into permission-winning categories. Missing causes remain specific evidence gaps even if a next-action string mentions budgets or policy. Dependency states are recorded facts, not a substituted decision cause.

Candidate presentation identifies invalidated, different-scope and recorded current-scope references without proving live Git validity. Existing action predicates, criteria confirmations, verification-gap acknowledgement and protected endpoints are retained. Manual dispatch still needs its exact preview/profile/revision/fingerprint; workflow-owned issues still suppress replacement manual actions. Fresh issue reads invalidate old lineage/criterion confirmations, exact issue/project/revision/scope mismatches are not displayed, and a failed list refresh fences its earlier pending detail response. Session IDs are shown without copying session objectives/notes; unavailable issue audit history is explicit rather than fabricated from task output. New public error summaries do not render raw source exceptions.

Workflow evidence has three accessible sections for operating state/trigger, scope/classification/testing/review requirements, and exact candidate/bounded correction. Writers in the recorded verification phase show Checking, not Working. Handoffs, attempts, receipts and review evidence retain their existing exact references. Incomplete accounting/correction lineage remains unknown/partial, not zero or additional capacity. Task detail separates task lifecycle/result, recorded scope/admission requirements, infrastructure receipts, declared model tests and verification. Receipt references do not claim a valid seal, passing tests, current Git, live authority or human acceptance. Legacy metadata alone cannot establish workflow ownership. Source observation uses stored `updated_at`, never polling time; overlapping task responses are sequence-bound with the unchanged 3000-ms interval. Existing explicit detail results/diffs/events remain in their existing local detail rather than being exported into new summaries or model context.

## T007 implementation and acceptance checkpoint - 2026-10-16

The owner's request authorized only `dashboard/src/ui/views/TodayView.tsx`, its test, `dashboard/src/ui/App.tsx` and its test, plus this design, `.agents/specs/operating-views.spec.yaml` and `.agents/specs/tasks/phase-q-operating-views.tasks.md`. No CSS/NewTaskForm/API/client/backend/model/schema/store/runtime/guard/cap/timeout/dependency/Harness scaffold/pin/validator behavior changed.

### Implemented behavior and explicit limits

- Every recommendation exposes project/signal, its own objective when supplied, the project's distinct strategic objective, suggested action and separate signal-reference/observation times. A deadline reference may be in the future; it is not mislabeled as freshness or renewed at render. External GitHub issue numbers are not local issue IDs. Missing objective data alone does not prove intentional personal independence.
- A user explicitly consults existing local operating-project and registered-profile GETs. Reads start only on that action, settle independently and reject foreign/malformed responses. Only exact same-project task/issue/workflow bindings expose local links; missing records are not replaced by a recent or similarly named item. Source availability, freshness, coverage, observation time and off-page records remain visible. The operating signals adapter is unavailable; this UI does not pretend to reconcile it with the Today feed.
- Personal/experimental independence is stated only when the canonical snapshot confirms it. Recorded execution/workflow roles and registered profiles are not current execution permission, selection, sandbox, task-kind, scope, consent or budget approval. No workflow is guessed from title/action text. Existing form/preview authorization remains required. Snapshot requests and role results are generation-fenced across signal changes/unmount; raw read/navigation exceptions stay private.
- Typed project/issue/task/workflow/objective navigation retains immediate return paths and a clear return to Today; sidebar changes clear stale focus/prefills. Editor launch is explicitly labeled. The actual project ID `surtec` uses project detail, while organization/objective navigation has a separate callback. Objective signals open their exact objective where known.
- All Today task prefills use the same generic instruction, including null/personal/experimental kinds: no signal reason, suggested action, title, evidence, feedback or confidential note is copied. Before opening the form, App confirms the exact project in existing dispatch options. An absent/unregistered target or failed read opens that project's detail with a public warning, without substituting the first available project. Late option reads cannot override another navigation or unmounted App. This read is not a reservation or atomic freeze across subsequent form reads/current-policy validation; no form or server guard changed.

## T008 implementation and acceptance checkpoint - 2026-10-16

The owner's continuation authorized only `dashboard/src/ui/components/ProjectReadinessPanel.tsx`, its test, `dashboard/src/ui/views/ProjectDetailView.tsx`, its test, `dashboard/src/ui/App.tsx` and its test. ### Implemented behavior and limits

- The compact project panel separates a discovered checkout/configured agents from contained-checkout/static mode options, reused integration observations, configured verification and saved Harness evidence. Its explicit lookup calls only existing operating-project GET (six rows per section) and dispatch options. Reads settle independently; failed/malformed/foreign/late results cannot replace another project's facts. No fetch or provider refresh is added on render. Project integrations are reset, bound to their exact project and generation-fenced across refresh/project changes. Session components are keyed per project so drafts/associations do not carry over.
- Readiness means ready to inspect, not authorized to execute. Read-only mode availability, sandbox ceilings and verification command presence do not establish logical-role/task-kind/scope/context/consent/budget admission. Missing checkout/no agents are concrete configuration gaps. No verification commands does not itself block analysis; optional objective/client/Harness absence does not disable personal navigation. Dispatch options expose no observation time. Operating-source observation/freshness/coverage, unknown totals and off-page evidence remain visible rather than claiming zero blockers or renewed freshness.
- Saved Harness status/validation is not a current receipt, ownership, installation failure or proven validity. The lookup reads the saved operating adapter; it does not invoke the inspector. Existing Harness detail still owns its normal inspection/explicit refresh. Native anchors focus existing Harness/session sections without another read or mutation. Session objectives/notes, raw source labels/summaries, instructions, paths and read exceptions are not copied into readiness.
- Exact canonical issue/workflow/task references open existing detail/preview/review paths. App preserves immediate task-to-Issues return, the selected project and existing Today/objective origins; stale or foreign project callbacks cannot replace them. Sidebar changes clear the context. The existing Issues list stays unfiltered and its form is not prefilled; the return banner instructs explicit project selection. There is no duplicate issue/task/session manager, automatic preview, authorization, correction, acceptance, publication or execution.
- The daily-loop explanation connects inspection/registration, choosing/proposing an issue, protected preview/scope authorization, exact-candidate verification/review and explicit session result/next step. Existing criteria, consent, scope and policy gates remain authoritative. Completed tasks, healthy CI/URLs and manual session closure cannot imply technical acceptance or delivery. Production budgets remain `not-enabled`; pending routines and continuity/backup/restore/recovery remain `deferred`, without activation actions.

Branch `codex/phase-q-operating-views`, HEAD `53ed01fd23bf62f1f08381b48f74b70595ef33f5` and the empty index are preserved. Existing dirty P/Q work and private roadmap prompts remain preserved and unpublished.

### Modified paths

- Contracts/state: `lib/issues/types.ts`, `schemas/issue-evidence.schema.json`, `lib/state/validation.ts`, `lib/state/issues.ts`, new `lib/state/issues.test.ts`.
- Service/API/client: `dashboard/src/server/issue-service.ts`, its existing test, `dashboard/src/server/operating-view-api.test.ts`, `dashboard/src/server/index.ts`, `dashboard/src/ui/api.ts`.
- Continuation/evidence: `.agents/specs/operating-views.spec.yaml`, `.agents/specs/tasks/phase-q-operating-views.tasks.md`, this design.

### Behavior and safety

An absent delivery facet remains absent rather than inferred readiness or success. Explicit null, missing/extra fields, unknown versions, invalid times, unsafe references and non-plain in-memory fields fail closed without repair. Strict facet checks run before the aggregate validator can invoke a getter; proxies, inherited or non-enumerable facts cannot serialize into an incomplete accepted record. Historical exact candidate/scope facts remain readable after later invalidation or scope change.

The protected no-store POST accepts at most 8 KiB and requires confirmation plus current issue/project/scope revision. A supplied candidate must be the exact current candidate; all-null remains explicitly unbound. Optional commitment references must already be linked and match the same project in a nofollow, identity/size/time-stable, bounded, strict UTF-8 local snapshot. No source reference is fetched or executed and no other store is locked/written; this is not a cross-store transaction. Active queued/running issue tasks and nonterminal workflow ownership refuse recording, without changing their existing progress guards.

Each success appends one informational work-product with null task/execution IDs and no testing fingerprint, plus an ID/kind-only event and the normal issue revision/updated-at. Lifecycle, scope, candidate, task ownership, grants, correction allowance and commitment state stay unchanged. Private summary/source/next action exist only in the nested facet; constant outer metadata cannot masquerade as passed verification/review or leak feedback into existing overview/prefill/implicit model context. Limits are 500/300/500 characters and 64 manual facts per issue under existing issue/global/event caps, with no eviction. Local state remains subject to the existing host-account protections, not application encryption.

### Behavior, privacy and bounds

The same panel exposes technical candidate/verification/review references separately from readiness, environment, handoff, response and follow-up observations. Local Done, a role review, a passing build, a healthy URL or completed commitment cannot fill another fact. Review references alone do not prove human acceptance; no Git inspection or current-receipt revalidation is claimed. Follow-up metadata has no machine-readable open/closed state, so its text never closes an issue/commitment. Personal projects do not require client/release ceremony.

Project and commitment mounting performs no delivery fetch or write. Explicit consultation reads the existing bounded issue list, filters exact project/commitment links and shows at most 50 choices with a partial-coverage notice. Exact issue selection rereads current lineage and revision/scope; unavailable, mismatched, future or malformed facets remain a public evidence gap. Historical observations retain their own candidate/scope/time rather than inheriting current readiness. Future scope is rejected consistently with T009.

Private fields are absent from cards, summaries, prefills and initial facts. Each record requires explicit private reveal; references are text only, never links, probes or commands. The new form requires the actual local observation time (converted to UTC), bounded fields, intentional nullable candidate/commitment bindings and unchecked confirmation. Changes to fields/project/issue/revision/scope/candidate fingerprint or invalidation reset the draft/confirmation. A ref mutex, generation fences, closed response binding and fresh lineage reread prevent stale presentation and duplicate UI submissions; unknown outcomes require explicit refresh rather than automatic POST replay. Existing T009 server guards remain authoritative.

Successful issue recording refreshes revision/lineage and invalidates previous context/criteria. Project/commitment views remain independent and do not update commitment state, execution authority or task instructions. Commitment context uses the saved record, never an unsaved project draft. Styles are delivery-prefixed: content-width buttons, readable native options, fieldset containment, visible keyboard focus and a one-column mobile fact layout.

## T012 bounded test-fixture cost reduction proposal - 2026-10-17 UTC

Status: **design only, awaiting implementation approval**. The owner requested a bounded correction proposal after the resumed timeout, with no guard/timeout change, added profiling or new campaign. This preparation changes only this design document. It neither repairs the suite nor closes T012; Q remains unaccepted and the existing null waiver is unchanged.

### Evidence and deliberately limited claim

The latest protected `pnpm test` attempt exceeded its existing 900-second limit; its launcher discarded child output. No fresh failing test, completed-suite duration or exclusive timeout cause is known. Historical mixed-run cache timings cannot establish current causality or predict a PASS.

Static inspection nevertheless identifies redundant setup that can be removed without replacing the operations under test:

| Existing fixture | Expanded cases using it | Initial Git processes now | Proposed seed construction |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `runner/worktree.test.ts` common `beforeEach` | 42 | 5 per case; 210 total | One pristine baseline; 5 processes |
| `runner/run-task.test.ts` `testingFixture` only | 13 | 5 per case; 65 total | Three exact content baselines; 15 processes |

The gross reduction is approximately **255 initial Git process launches**, before the additional helper regression tests. Physical copy/validation/cleanup still cost time. This is a concrete setup-cost reduction, not a measured speedup, proof of the timeout's cause or guarantee of completion within 900 seconds. No new timer, benchmark, reporter, profiler or performance telemetry is proposed.

### Exact implementation scope proposed for approval

1. New `runner/testing/git-fixture.ts`: a small test-only pristine-baseline helper.
2. New `runner/testing/git-fixture.test.ts`: physical isolation, mismatch/refusal and owned-cleanup regressions.
3. `runner/worktree.test.ts`: replace only repeated initial repository preparation, attach the baseline after the existing `README.md` write, and add seed-lifetime cleanup.
4. `runner/run-task.test.ts`: replace only the five initial Git calls inside `testingFixture` and add seed-lifetime cleanup. The general mocked/read-only `beforeEach`, issue/context/budget setup and every test body remain unchanged.

This design may subsequently record the scoped result. No other implementation path is included. The final acceptance result would update the existing Q evidence artifacts only under separate execution authority; T012 is not checked off by focused tests.

### Pristine baseline and private-copy contract

- Each test file owns a separate lazy baseline factory and unique temporary seed root. There is no process-global shared cache, persistent cache, cross-worker writable seed or reuse of a case repository. Seeds contain only the same public synthetic files and initial Git metadata as today's setup, never normal state, sibling repositories, credentials, tasks, workflow records or evidence.
- Use one narrow operation: attach a physical copy of pristine `.git` metadata to a case's existing synthetic working tree. The destination repository must be the exact owned case path and contain the descriptor's exact regular-file inventory and bytes. Reject missing, extra, changed or redirected files. Accept `.git` only when absent or a real empty directory; remove an empty directory non-recursively, never overwrite a nonempty or redirected `.git`.
- A new seed is built from the descriptor using the existing fixture's five Git commands, arguments, identity, environment and hook protections. The worktree baseline retains `init -b main`, `t` / `t@t.t`, `README.md` containing `hi\n` and commit message `init`. Run-task baselines retain the previous default-branch behavior, `Synthetic` / `fixture@example.test`, message `base`, `managedGitArgs` and `localGitEnvironment`.
- The run-task descriptor includes the exact selected-project `AGENTS.md`, `src/value.json`, `verify.mjs` and optional `refactor.mjs`. Its three closed variants are value 0 without refactor, value 0 with refactor, and value 1 without refactor. Match paths, bytes and relevant file modes; reject a different descriptor under an existing key. `strict` and `verify` still configure each case's independent external registry/context policy; they do not create additional content variants or share that state.
- Publish a seed only after all construction Git commands terminate successfully. Copy regular files physically, including Git objects/index/refs/config, without `clone`, hardlinks, alternates, symlinks, junctions or a shared object database. Reject worktree/gitdir/commondir indirection, linked objects, pending locks and absolute case/seed path bindings in the seed. No live `.git/worktrees`, candidate checkout or custom case hook is reusable seed content. Seed metadata must be self-contained after the original seed root is removed.
- Never mutate a published seed or derive it from a previous case's result. A descriptor mismatch fails rather than silently rebuilding from untrusted case contents or falling back to another variant. Preserve existing file-writing calls; the helper changes only synthetic initial Git preparation, not runtime Git execution or validation.
- Keep the existing unique root and per-case cleanup. Dispose only the seed factory's exact owned, containment-checked root in `afterAll`, after per-case work is stopped. A failed setup still registers cleanup. Do not remove any historical interrupted fixture or any guessed temporary root. No new cleanup timeout, retries or ownership bypass is allowed.
- Do not add Git subprocess validation to every copy; use bounded local manifest/metadata checks there. The helper's focused regressions use real Git to prove copied repositories resolve to their own top-level/common directory, have the expected tracked baseline and retain no dependency on the seed. This is not a production Git cache or a replacement for product checks.

### Coverage and safety invariants

Preserve all existing case names, parameter rows, assertion meaning and timeouts. In particular, retain all 42 worktree cases and 13 run-task baseline cases, their real product Git/worktree/commit/verification operations, hook-denial paths, strict-TDD observations, policy revocation and candidate/issue acceptance assertions. No `skip`, `only`, reduced input matrix, fake replacement for real integration or changed test ordering/concurrency is permitted.

New helper coverage must prove two copies and the seed have independent file identities; mutating tracked bytes, index, objects, config, refs, hooks or generated worktrees in one cannot affect another or the seed. Removing the seed must leave an existing copy usable. Also cover all three run-task variants, exact `AGENTS.md` binding, descriptor mismatch, extra file, nonempty `.git`, unsafe link/indirection, contaminated seed and failed-setup cleanup. The tests must preserve a refusal target's original bytes and never follow a cleanup target outside the owned root.

Keep `pnpm test` as `vitest run`, every currently discovered test, `maxWorkers: 2`, pool/isolation defaults, existing browser opt-ins, all test/runtime/lease limits, the original four-check catalog and its 900-second ceiling unchanged. Existing workers, stores, `fsync`, locks, route guards, current-state rereads, maintenance ownership checks and capability enforcement remain outside scope.

`runner/workflow-orchestrator.test.ts`, all profiling code/tests and their historical prefix/full-file pins remain byte-identical. The profiler's existing run-task stop-variant path/names remain unchanged; those cases do not use the proposed baseline helper. No package, dependency, runtime, Harness skill/manifest/validator, approval metadata, P waiver or deferred production activation is included.

The state/execution safety skills ruled out caching validated production state or filesystem ownership: those rereads protect different boundaries. Reuse is limited to immutable synthetic preparation, with actual state and execution guards still exercised independently by every case.

### Proposed implementation and validation sequence; not executed

After explicit implementation approval, capture fresh hashes and preserve an inventory of all existing cases/parameter rows/assertions in the two files. Implement only the four targets and inspect the precise setup-only diff. Stop on unexpected baseline drift, a necessary additional target or any invariant mismatch, rather than expanding scope automatically.

Run once, in order, only after that approval:

```powershell
pnpm exec vitest run runner/testing/git-fixture.test.ts runner/worktree.test.ts runner/run-task.test.ts --reporter=verbose
# Only after a complete exit-0 result:
pnpm typecheck
```

Keep the established sanitized environment and 900-second outer bound, with every existing test timeout unchanged. Require a complete terminal result, all previous cases plus the new isolation cases, no newly skipped cases, actual owned cleanup and unchanged non-scope hashes. Stop at the first observed failure or unknown; no retry, full suite, CI, audit or browser campaign is implicitly authorized. Do not infer suite-level timing from this focused result.

Only a subsequent separately authorized final acceptance can close T012: the unchanged protected original Q catalog with `-TimeoutSeconds 900`, then, only on success, the existing CI (900-second bound), audit, dedicated Q browser smoke and general compatibility browser smoke, stopping at the first failure or unknown. Require complete fresh results and owned browser/process/fixture cleanup; focused or historical PASS cannot substitute for that sequence. Known P failures remain unresolved unless freshly demonstrated otherwise, and deferred P work stays deferred.

If the unchanged full gate still times out, record T012 as incomplete and stop. This proposal does not authorize another diagnostic/profiling loop, increased limits, a waiver or later phase. Rollback, if requested, is limited to this repair's own helper/setup edits while preserving all earlier dirty P/Q work; no destructive Git operation is proposed. No manual repeat is required for these repeatable checks, and no commit, push or merge is included.

## T012 bounded Vitest temporary-directory proposal - 2026-10-17 UTC

Status: **design only; execution awaits separate approval**. The owner approved the preceding four-file fixture implementation and one focused validation followed conditionally by typecheck. Those four files were implemented; static comparison preserved existing test bodies/assertions/timeouts and all other observed paths. The single focused command stopped during Vitest startup, before test execution, on `EPERM` while renaming `<host-temp>/<run-id>/ssr/.tmp-*` to its module hash. The supervisor returned exit 1 after 2.910 seconds, requested owned-tree termination and confirmed principal-child exit. No typecheck, retry or campaign followed; temporary-cache cleanup was not established and no historical temporary root was deleted.

This new request authorizes only the design below. No environment override, directory creation, sentinel/probe, test, compiler, validator or campaign runs during its preparation. The fixture implementation remains unvalidated, T012 stays unchecked, Q stays in progress and the null waiver is unchanged.

### Installed-source evidence and limits

Read-only inspection of installed Vitest **4.1.11**, rather than an assumed newer version, identifies the failing layer:

- `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js:13087` initializes `_tmpDir` as `join(tmpdir(), nanoid())`; the root project receives that path at line 11080. Other project constructors use the same `node:os.tmpdir()` basis at line 10707.
- `ModuleFetcher`, lines 1010-1029, creates temporary module copies for fork workers at `<tmpProjectDir>/<environment>/<sha1(module-id)>`, even when persistent filesystem module caching is not enabled. Lines 838-842 implement the observed `.tmp-*` write followed by `rename`.
- Vite `cacheDir`, Vitest's result cache and experimental filesystem module caching are different controls. Changing those would not select this temporary-copy root under the current configuration. Do not disable caching, enable an experimental cache or patch installed dependencies.
- `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/defaults.9aQKnqFk.js:6` already excludes `**/node_modules/**` and `**/.git/**`; the repository's `vitest.config.ts` does not replace that exclusion. Its `maxWorkers: 2`, test includes, pool/isolation defaults and existing setup remain unchanged.

The failed path was under the host's redirected sandbox temporary directory. That fact does **not** prove an ACL defect, antivirus interference, an open-handle race, old cache corruption or an inter-run collision. Vitest already assigns an invocation-specific random subdirectory. Moving the temporary root changes location and explicit ownership; it does not establish the cause or guarantee success. A basic filesystem check cannot reproduce every concurrent Vitest condition or prove the full suite fits within 900 seconds.

### Exact proposed scope: invocation environment only

Keep all four implemented files, every existing source/test/config/dependency, all Harness scripts/skills/pins, profiling artifacts and Q approval/completion metadata byte-identical. No new repository script, flag, reporter or permanent configuration is proposed. Use only an ephemeral extension of the existing focused-command launcher: create a private temporary root and override the **child's existing** `TEMP` and `TMP` entries after constructing its unchanged sanitized allowlist.

The proposed owned path is:

```text
<local-path><fresh-uuid>/
```

This stays within the already authorized workspace without an unsandboxed launch or a permission expansion. It is under an existing Git ignore and Vitest's existing discovery exclusion; no ignore/include/exclude change is needed. At design time, the workspace ancestors and `node_modules` inspected as ordinary directories, while `.cache` was absent. Revalidate those facts at execution; the observation is not a write-permission proof or continuing ownership guarantee.

Create `.cache` only if absent and safely contained; never replace a redirected or occupied path. Create the nonce root exclusively, record its canonical path and filesystem identity, and reject a pre-existing nonce, reparse/link ancestor or containment mismatch. The launcher owns only its exact nonce root, not `node_modules`, `.cache`, installed packages, another invocation or a historical cache. Do not create this root during design preparation.

Set both child `TEMP` and `TMP` to that one canonical path **before** starting Node/pnpm. Preserve `Environment.Clear()`, the same allowlist, `CI=1`, working directory, executable resolution, arguments, permissions, stop conditions, stream handling and 900-second outer bound. Do not mutate the parent process's environment or user/machine settings, introduce inherited secrets/`NODE_OPTIONS`, change HOME/CODEX_HOME, alter ACLs, disable antivirus/security, elevate, or retry under another policy.

This relocates **all `os.tmpdir()` consumers in those children**, including the synthetic Git fixtures and seed factories, not just Vitest's module copies. No claim of separate cache/fixture roots is made. Native `node_modules` exclusion prevents temporary test copies from expanding discovery, but a focused PASS is not proof that every full-suite fixture behaves identically at a different path. Do not propagate this environment automatically to a full gate, CI or another workflow.

### Proposed execution sequence after approval; not executed

1. Capture fresh hashes and confirm the four-file implementation plus all non-scope controls still match their last observed closure. Recheck the installed Vitest source/config contract and the proposed path's canonical ownership. Stop on drift or an unsupported layout; do not repin or choose another directory automatically.
2. Create only the owned nonce root. Using the same Node executable and sanitized child environment, run a minimal filesystem preflight **without importing Vitest or tests**: require `os.tmpdir()` to resolve to that root; inside a dedicated owned sentinel subdirectory, write a fixed public value to a new temporary file, rename it to a new destination, verify bytes, write another value to a different temporary file, rename over that same destination and verify the replacement. Remove only those sentinel artifacts after success. Keep the existing 900-second outer bound, add no retry/delay/timeout, telemetry, timing instrumentation or stress loop. Any refusal, nonzero/unknown result or unexpected identity stops the entire sequence before tests.
3. Only if preflight completes successfully, execute **once**, with the same child-only temporary override and unchanged test timeouts:

   ```powershell
   pnpm exec vitest run runner/testing/git-fixture.test.ts runner/worktree.test.ts runner/run-task.test.ts --reporter=verbose
   ```

4. Only after a complete exit-0 focused result, execute **once** `pnpm typecheck` with the same sanitized child-only temporary root and existing outer bound. No test retry, full `pnpm test`, protected catalog, CI, audit, browser smoke or profiler campaign is included. Stop at the first observed failure or unknown. A repeated `EPERM` is reported, not caught and ignored, retried with sleeps, worked around by lowering concurrency or followed by an automatic fallback.
5. Confirm owned writers have ended, then revalidate the nonce root's identity and containment before deleting only that exact directory. If writer shutdown, identity or containment is unknown, retain it and report cleanup as unconfirmed; never delete a guessed root. Do not clean the earlier redirected temporary directory, other Vitest caches or either parent directory. Finish with read-only source/non-scope integrity checks and report preflight, focused, typecheck and cleanup separately, distinguishing not run from PASS.

The execution-safety skill keeps environment authority, process termination and cleanup ownership separate from test success. No repeated manual validation is delegated. An approved and successful focused sequence would validate the fixture repair only; original Q final acceptance, its unchanged 900-second checks and subsequent conditional CI/audit/browser sequence still require separate authorization and actual complete results. Neither this design nor a future preflight/focused PASS closes T012, repairs the original full-suite timeout, waives deferred P work or starts another phase. No commit, push or merge is included.

## T012 bounded T007 timeout diagnostic proposal - 2026-10-17 UTC

Status: **design only; no implementation or repeated test authorized by this preparation**. The owner approved the preceding child-only temporary-directory sequence. Its filesystem preflight passed, including new-name rename, replacement rename, exact bytes and sentinel cleanup. One three-file focused run then started without an observed recurrence of the startup EPERM and produced 32 passing test lines before the first failure: `runTask > T007 relays independently proven tokens stop before terminal task ownership is released`, `Test timed out in 5000ms`, with a native reported case duration of 6100 ms. The unchanged supervisor stopped at that failure after 29.506 seconds, requested tree termination and confirmed principal-child exit. These are partial observations, not completed-file/suite results. Typecheck, retries and campaigns were not run.

The owned temporary nonce from that attempt remains retained because shutdown of all writers was not independently established; its identity still matched at closure. Do not reuse, mutate or delete it for this diagnostic. The four-file Git fixture repair remains incompletely validated, T012 is unchecked and Q is not accepted. The prior preflight and startup do not establish the original EPERM's cause or a general fix.

### Static findings and hypotheses

- `runner/run-task.test.ts:272-307` constructs `governedBudgetFixture` synchronously: context and issue authorization, workflow preview/start, task budget reservation/commit/claim/bind and workflow claim. The case then tightens its task cap, configures an immediately resolved fake executor, awaits `runTask` once, performs synchronous assertions and calls both proof releases. It does not start a scheduler/heartbeat or invoke `testingFixture` or the new Git baseline helper. The tokens variant does not enable fake timers.
- `runner/task-orchestrator.ts:356-404` declares budget observation/settlement async, but their inspected bodies perform synchronous transactions without an internal await. The independent stop path in `runner/run-task.ts:344-369` and `runner/workflow-orchestrator.ts:110-140` retains exact budget/workflow/issue authority and releases the workflow mutation before the issue mutation. Inspected locks fail or retry immediately; no sleeping lock-wait loop was found. This is not proof that native I/O cannot block or that no other fault exists.
- Installed `@vitest/runner` **4.1.11**, `dist/chunk-artifact.js:2261-2318`, enforces both a timer and an elapsed-time check when the test's promise resolves. Lines 2288-2293 can reject an already completed microtask chain that exceeded the same timeout. Therefore the timeout message alone does not distinguish an unfinished await from accumulated synchronous work, post-run assertions or a body that completed over budget.
- Synchronous filesystem validation/replacement, fixture admission, `runTask`, assertions and concurrent load are candidate contributors, not measured causes. No production guard, state reread, fsync, ownership check or assertion may be removed to test that hypothesis. The absence of a direct Git-helper call also does not rule out indirect interaction with the parallel run.

### Minimal proposed change: native failure context, not a profiler

Implementation would touch **only `runner/run-task.test.ts`**, plus this document for the scoped result if requested. Import the installed native `onTestFailed` API and register one failure-only handler inside the existing parameterized test **only for `dimension === "tokens"`**. Keep the current `it.each` declaration, all three rows, test names, original fake result/usage, spy behavior, assertions, await and release order unchanged.

Use one test-local closed categorical value, initially `entered`, updated only after these existing boundaries return:

```text
entered -> fixture-created -> run-armed -> fake-result-built
        -> run-returned -> assertions-complete -> release-returned
```

`fake-result-built` means only that the existing fake result object was constructed, not that a real model or runtime ran. `run-returned` follows the original await; `assertions-complete` follows all existing assertions; `release-returned` follows both existing release calls. Do not move, duplicate, cache or short-circuit their work. Assignments are tokens-conditional so runtime/unknown variants gain no diagnostic behavior.

On failure, emit at most **one short `console.error` line** containing a fixed diagnostic tag, the literal tokens variant and that closed categorical value. Emit nothing on success. Do not serialize the test context, errors, task records, proofs, manifests, prompts, paths or persisted state. Apart from that single native failure callback, do not add clocks, durations, counts, timestamps, performance hooks, spans, sampling, tracing streams, observers, polling, event/process listeners, timers, new scripts/reporters or profiling dependencies. The existing native error remains authoritative and unmodified; the handler neither catches nor replaces it.

Installed runner lines 2973-2988 execute `onTestFailed` **after** `afterEach` and fixture cleanup. The handler must therefore read only its own local primitive value, never global `root`, files, stores, proofs or restored spies. The value is the last boundary observed when that handler runs, not a timestamped snapshot at the precise 5000-ms deadline. A `release-returned` value with a native timeout would be consistent with the runner's over-budget completion check; it would not prove where time was spent or absolve another operation.

Do not throw another diagnostic error. Send the one closed line directly through Vitest's existing captured console output. Even so, the supervisor's unchanged first-failure termination and stream drain cannot guarantee its delivery. A missing/malformed line after failure is classified as unavailable diagnostic evidence, not a guessed phase or permission to repeat automatically.

Moving the expensive fixture into `beforeEach`/`beforeAll` is explicitly excluded: that would move work outside the current test body's 5000-ms boundary. Adding prefix tests, splitting the original test to distribute its time budget, sharing authority/state, adding sleeps, raising hook/test limits or lowering workers is also excluded. This proposal adds error context only; it is not a timeout repair.

### Proposed one-shot diagnostic sequence after explicit approval

1. Capture fresh hashes and preserve all current T007 names, rows, assertions, ordering and limits. Apply only the import, tokens-only native failure handler and categorical assignments above. Review the exact diff; any needed additional code target or semantic change stops the slice.
2. Use a **fresh** owned nonce and the same previously approved sanitized child-only `TEMP/TMP` procedure, filesystem preflight, executable/working directory, supervision and 900-second outer bound. No parent/global environment change, permission expansion or modification of the retained earlier nonce is included. A failed/unknown preflight stops before tests.
3. Execute exactly one diagnostic selection, not a phase acceptance substitute:

   ```powershell
   pnpm exec vitest run runner/run-task.test.ts -t 'T007 relays independently proven tokens stop before terminal task ownership is released$' --reporter=verbose
   ```

   Require exactly the intended tokens case to execute. Other name-filtered cases are not newly disabled in source and are not counted as accepted. Preserve the original 5000-ms test limit, fake-protocol behavior, configuration, `maxWorkers: 2`, pool defaults and all runtime limits. Stop at the first observed failure or unknown; preserve native failure and any delivered categorical line separately. No automatic pattern fallback, retry, deliberate new failure or second diagnostic follows.
4. Only after a complete selected-case PASS, run `pnpm typecheck` once under the same sanitized environment and unchanged outer bound. A one-case PASS means the failure was not reproduced in that isolated invocation; it does not prove the cause was concurrency, validate the other variants/three files or resolve the full-suite timeout. No broader rerun is conditionally authorized here.
5. Apply the existing owned-writer shutdown, root identity/containment and cleanup rules. Unknown writer shutdown retains the new nonce and reports cleanup unconfirmed. Source outside the single proposed file, all guards/timeouts, fixture helpers, schemas, dependencies, Harness artifacts/pins, profilers and Q completion/approval metadata remain unchanged.

State/execution safety skills preserve the real authority checks and avoid forensic reads of a deleted, changing or misattributed fixture after teardown. No retained-state reader, migration, product runtime call, paid/provider smoke, full suite, original catalog, CI, audit, browser campaign, commit, push or merge is proposed or executed in this design turn. The next action requires approval of this diagnostic implementation and one-shot sequence, not approval of Q or another phase.

## T012 bounded T007 fixture-cost reduction proposal - 2026-10-17

Status: **design only; implementation and further checks require approval**. The subsequent authorized tokens-only diagnostic had a successful temporary-filesystem preflight, then failed at the unchanged 5000-ms test limit (native case duration 5577 ms). Its one failure-context line was `[T007 failure context] tokens: release-returned`. The supervisor stopped at the first observed failure after 11.845 seconds and confirmed principal-child exit; typecheck and all subsequent checks were not run. This establishes that the original assertions and both releases returned before the post-teardown failure callback, not that they returned within the deadline or which operation consumed the time. Both historical temporary roots remain retained with all-writer shutdown unconfirmed; this proposal does not inspect, reuse or remove them.

### Evidence and selected change

`runner/run-task.test.ts` currently copies the entire canonical role registry and all its required evaluation files in `contextFixture`. It contains six roles and seven distinct evaluation references. Every real `readRoles` call in `lib/policy/roles.ts` rereads and validates the required evaluations of **all configured roles**, even when selecting only `portfolio-analyst`. T007's `analysis` workflow selects exactly that role and `product-manager`; the canonical role currently requires only `evals/v1/cases/product-manager-pass.json`.

The unchanged `schemas/agent-role-registry.schema.json` permits one role. `resolveWorkflowAssignments` authorizes the roles of the selected template, not every supported template. Current context policy has no routes or per-role testing entries; if that changes to reference an omitted role, the real `readRoles` cross-reference check must continue to reject it. These are static findings, not timing measurements or a proven explanation for the timeout.

Propose implementation in **only `runner/run-task.test.ts`**, with a scoped result in this document if requested:

1. Give `contextFixture` a closed `"full" | "budget-analysis"` mode, defaulting to `"full"`. Preserve the existing full-mode preparation and every existing caller's behavior. No free-form role list, path, policy or runtime option is introduced.
2. In the opt-in mode, derive a private registry from the current canonical source, retaining its root schema version and the **entire unchanged** `portfolio-analyst` object. Require exactly one matching role; absence, duplication or an unsupported mode fails instead of choosing a fallback. Preserve all role fields, including agent membership, task kinds, sandbox/tool capabilities, approvals, budget policy, handoff contracts, review depth, required skills and every required evaluation reference. Do not hand-author a permissive replacement or silently truncate the evaluation list if it grows.
3. Copy the retained role's evaluation files unchanged with the existing copy loop. Keep `agents.yml`, `context-policy.yml`, `workflow-policy.yml`, project registration, synthetic project rules, issue scope/authorization, request, previews, budget source/caps and fake executor result unchanged. Only the test-owned role registry and its unneeded evaluation copies differ; canonical repository configuration remains untouched. New task/context fingerprints must be produced and checked by the real existing flow, never patched, cached or prebuilt.
4. Give `governedBudgetFixture` the same closed mode with the full default and forward it to `contextFixture`. Opt in **only** at the fixture call inside the existing parameterized T007 `tokens/runtime/unknown` group. The neighboring issue-write-fault/high-water case and every other caller retain the full registry by default. Do not move setup into a hook or outside the current timed test body.
5. Preserve every existing assertion and its execution order, including workflow-service `detail`, both adjacent task reads, issue/event/ledger checks, negative effect checks and both proof releases. Preserve the existing tokens-only categorical failure context exactly; add no further diagnostic instrumentation.

At the current configuration, each real role-registry authorization in those three cases would validate one role and read/parse one evaluation instead of six roles and seven evaluations; setup would likewise copy one evaluation instead of seven. This removes six unrelated evaluation reads per `readRoles` invocation without skipping any call, loop or guard over the configured role. The number of invocations and elapsed savings are unmeasured. No claim is made that this eliminates the observed timeout, resolves the original full-suite timeout or guarantees PASS.

This deliberately narrows the synthetic registry universe of these budget-relay cases; they will no longer incidentally detect a broken evaluation belonging only to an unrelated role. It does **not** narrow the selected role's policy or remove an assertion. All full-mode callers retain that incidental full-registry validation, and the neighboring full-mode case is included in the proposed focused control. Do not describe the fixtures as byte-identical or claim unchanged coverage of unrelated role configuration within these three cases.

### Preserved boundaries and rejected alternatives

All real preview/start, context admission, policy rereads, budget reservation/commit, task claim/bind, workflow claim, current-scope/lease/proof checks, independent stop evidence, canonical validation/locking/atomic replacement and releases remain unchanged. No mocks of policy/store/authority readers, runtime or store edits, new dependency, migration, shared mutable fixture, cache, scheduler, paid/provider call or capability expansion is proposed. Readiness and DTO assertions stay in place; replacing `detail` with a narrower store read is intentionally excluded to preserve that integration coverage.

The test remains one body per original parameter, with the same names, all rows, 5000-ms test boundary, hooks, fake Date behavior, worker/pool configuration and 900-second outer ceiling. No split test, skipped assertion, relaxed cap, increased timeout, lower concurrency, background warmup, sleep/retry, new reporter, metric, profiling or Harness/catalog/pin change is included. Existing Git-fixture repairs remain incompletely validated; this proposal cannot certify them.

### Proposed validation after approval; not executed

1. Capture fresh source/control hashes and review the exact single-file implementation diff. Confirm the canonical selected role and copied evaluation bytes are unchanged; all original assertion/await/release statements and failure context are preserved; only the parameterized group's fixture call opts in. Stop on drift, unexpected policy references or a need for another target; do not rewrite policy to fit the fixture.
2. Use a fresh owned nonce with the previously bounded sanitized child-only `TEMP/TMP` procedure and filesystem preflight. Keep environment allowlist, permissions, executable, working directory, process supervision, stop conditions and the 900-second ceiling unchanged. Do not touch either retained nonce or alter parent/user/machine settings. Failed or unknown preflight stops before tests.
3. Execute exactly once, preserving all native test timeouts:

   ```powershell
   pnpm exec vitest run runner/run-task.test.ts -t 'T007 (relays independently proven (tokens|runtime|unknown) stop before terminal task ownership is released|retains the first independent stop across an issue-write fault and later global high-water)$' --reporter=verbose
   ```

   Require all four intended cases and a complete exit-0 result: three focused-registry variants and one unchanged full-registry control. Other name-filtered tests are not counted as validated. Stop at the first observed failure or unknown, retaining native errors and any existing categorical line; no automatic retry or alternate selection follows.
4. Only after that complete focused PASS, execute `pnpm typecheck` once with the same scoped environment and outer ceiling. Stop at its first failure or unknown. Apply existing owned-writer shutdown, identity/containment and cleanup rules; retain and report the new nonce if shutdown or ownership is uncertain. Finish with read-only scope/hash checks and separate preflight/focused/typecheck/cleanup outcomes.

No tests, preflight, typecheck, profiling, validators or campaigns run during this design preparation. A future focused PASS validates only this repair and its four selected cases. Resuming the original unchanged Q gate and conditional full acceptance remains a separate authorized step after reporting those results; no automatic full suite, CI, audit, Q/general browser smoke, commit, push, merge or later phase is included. T012 stays unchecked, Q remains in progress and deferred P work remains deferred. The execution-safety and file-state skills keep all authority and durable-state validation in the real path while limiting the proposed reduction to unrelated synthetic fixture input.
