## T012 bounded concrete test-error capture proposal - 2026-10-17

Status: **design only; no diagnostic execution or implementation authorized by this preparation**. The latest original gate returned exit 1 at `ac-behavior` / `pnpm test`, observed between 12:52:40 and 12:53:39 UTC. It did not report a whole-check timeout. Its individual failures, counts and cause remain unavailable because the unchanged Harness discards both child streams. Earlier T007/temporary-cache/P failures must not be substituted for that missing evidence. T012 remains unchecked and Q is not accepted.

### Static basis and minimal scope

- Keep `.agents/scripts/common.ps1`, `validate-spec.ps1`, the original spec/catalog, scripts, dependencies, tests, source, configuration, Harness pins and completion/approval metadata unchanged. Do not teach the exact catalog a diagnostic command or enable a persistent log option. This proposal changes only how a separately approved, ephemeral diagnostic invocation observes existing output; this document is the sole repository edit in this design turn.
- Installed Vitest **4.1.11**, `vitest/dist/chunks/index.UpGiHP7g.js:3997-4008`, implements native `VerboseReporter.onTestCaseResult` by logging the failed case title and then each `error.message` with an arrow in the same callback. The concrete message is not restricted to the final summary. Full error frames/diffs and some module/setup/unhandled errors are reported separately; a case-level immediate message cannot be promised for every failure type.
- The native TAP/TAP-flat alternatives emit only from `onTestRunEnd` in this installation; they do not solve early capture. No custom reporter, JSON report, output file, profiler, test hook or added task-location flag is proposed. Native verbose output is ordinary failure evidence, not execution timing attribution.
- The previous ephemeral supervisor can stop on a failure marker and drain remaining pipe data without exposing it wholesale. Its immediate stop, 900-second execution ceiling, existing 5-second principal-exit wait and 3-second post-stop drain remain unchanged. Do not delay tree termination until a later message arrives, wait for the full run summary, lengthen those bounds or silently allow the suite to finish after a known failure.

### Proposed one-shot diagnostic after separate approval

Execute exactly once through the installed Node/package-manager entry, with the same working directory and sanitized environment as the original gate:

```powershell
pnpm test --reporter=verbose
```

This is the full native test selection, with no file/name filters, bail, retry, skip, worker/pool change, fake-timer change, test/hook timeout change or additional runtime opt-in. Preserve the existing environment allowlist and `CI=1`; retain the original `TEMP/TMP` values. Do not reuse the focused-only override or create a nonce/sentinel. No parent/user/machine environment, security policy, ACL, cache, dependency or retained-root modification is included.

The ephemeral parent's only proposed implementation delta is a bounded, in-memory first-error projection instead of forwarding raw stdout/stderr:

1. Drain both streams concurrently while recognizing native case, suite-load or startup failure evidence. Keep per-stream ordering and the binding between a native failed-case header and its immediately attached message; do not associate an arbitrary stderr line, another case or user console text merely because it arrived nearby. Strip ANSI framing only for recognition. Buffer only a bounded candidate/error tail (at most 64 KiB per stream, 8 KiB per line); overflow or an unsupported/ambiguous format is an unknown result, never an unbounded raw dump.
2. At the first observed failure or unknown condition, stop using the existing termination path immediately. Parse only the first candidate plus data already buffered or received during the existing post-stop drain. Native verbose emits title/message synchronously, but process termination, buffering or stream interleaving can still prevent delivery. Preserve that limitation explicitly: an absent/incomplete message is **unknown**, not a guessed error, reason to wait longer or permission to rerun.
3. Emit at most one small safe record, capped at 2 KiB: evidence kind, a repository-relative test path verified against the public source inventory, a publicly verified case title where available and a safe message projection. Include a native error class/code only when actually emitted and admitted by the public vocabulary; otherwise leave it null/unavailable. Verbose exposes `error.message`, not necessarily `error.name`; do not infer a native class from an assertion-like phrase. Coordinates are optional only when actually emitted or subsequently located by read-only source inspection; do not fabricate them or claim the full stack/diff was captured. A startup/module error can have no identified test case. Output remains untrusted diagnostic evidence, not proof of root cause or execution authority.
4. Use existing `lib/security/redaction.ts` before emitting any admitted text. Load it only in the ephemeral observer through the already installed TypeScript support; do not inject a loader or Node option into the test child. Failure to initialize the observer/redactor stops before the test command. The redactor is not a general privacy guarantee: project only messages fully matching public framework/repository error templates with typed numeric/code slots, never a public prefix plus an arbitrary suffix. Examples are a native timeout and its numeric limit, a filesystem/module error code and operation with paths withheld, or an exact public contract-error literal. For assertion failures, retain only public identity and an admitted public error description, not arbitrary compared values. An unsupported message is suppressed and explicitly unavailable, not described as a captured exact error.
5. Do not publish or persist full streams, snapshots, task/context/proof objects, credentials, instructions, raw reasoning, private strings, absolute/UNC paths, URLs with auth, source snippets, actual/expected objects, diffs or arbitrary stacks. The final record cannot exceed its bounds by embedding a truncated private blob. Use unknown/suppressed fields instead of a raw fallback; discard the in-memory raw tail after projection. If only file/name is safely available, report **identity captured; concrete message unavailable**, not diagnostic success.

This capture envelope adds no product guard/cap or test condition and removes none. It does not catch/replace assertions or errors, alter exit status, pause tests for inspection, collect metrics, add clocks/spans/timestamps to tests, inspect caches/normal state, or read the retained historical roots. The existing outer supervisor clock remains process supervision only. Process-tree termination, principal exit, stream closure and fixture cleanup remain separately reported; an exited principal is not proof that all fixture writers stopped.

### Approval and stopping sequence

1. After approval only, capture fresh hashes, confirm the installed reporter contract and source/catalog/environment controls, and prepare/review the ephemeral observer against the limits above. No permanent source/test/validator/helper file is added. An unexpected layout, changed control or unsupported observer stops before execution; do not perform a probing test launch or broaden the target set.
2. Run the one native verbose diagnostic above with the unchanged 900-second ceiling and fail/unknown-first supervisor. Retain only the permitted first-error record plus native terminal/process evidence. Do not count partial passing lines as a completed suite. Record missing data and unconfirmed cleanup honestly, with no guessed path removal or automatic fallback.
3. Stop after that invocation regardless of outcome. No second test, focused reproduction, typecheck, schema/build check, original gate, CI, audit or browser campaign follows automatically. A complete exit-0 diagnostic means only that the failure was not reproduced in that invocation; it does not retroactively pass the original exact-catalog gate. A concrete failure permits a scoped repair design, not an automatic implementation. Unknown capture permits a further human decision, not a repeated campaign.

The execution-safety and file-state skills keep real authority/validation intact and avoid turning output capture into state inspection or repair. No execution, parser/observer implementation, preflight sentinel, test, validator, typecheck, smoke or profiling ran in this design preparation. The next requested approval is for the ephemeral capture preparation and **one diagnostic invocation only**, not Q acceptance or another phase. No commit, push, merge or publication is included.

Design-only closure: source hashes covered 1229 tracked or non-ignored paths; only this document changed, the other 1228 were unchanged and none disappeared. Whitespace and independent static reporter/privacy review found no blocker; native class availability and full-template matching were clarified. Branch/HEAD and the empty index were preserved. No retained temporary root was read or changed.

## T012 bounded module-error capture design - 2026-10-17; proposed

The owner requested a design to capture module errors after the unknown diagnostic above. This is a **design-only** checkpoint: no observer implementation, parser check, probe, test, validator, typecheck or campaign is authorized by this request. The sole repository edit is this proposal. T012 remains unchecked and Q remains unaccepted; no deferred P work, approval or acceptance condition changes.

### Read-only findings and limits

Three defects are visible in the previous in-memory observer, without reconstructing its discarded input:

1. Its module identity expression admits a bare `FAIL <file>` but not the bracketed relative-path suffix emitted by the installed reporter. A native header can therefore trigger termination while leaving the public file unavailable.
2. Its post-stop association handles a failed case's adjacent arrow message, but never assigns a module's subsequent error line. Fixing the path expression alone cannot capture the concrete module error.
3. Its unanchored PowerShell `-match '\bFAIL\b'` is case-insensitive. A successful case containing `fail closed` can be misclassified as a module failure. Such public titles exist in `runner/workflow-orchestrator.test.ts` and `dashboard/src/server/index.test.ts`. This is a possible false positive, **not evidence that it caused the previous unknown result**; that header was not retained.

Installed Vitest **4.1.11** supplies the applicable native contract, inspected without execution:

- `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/index.UpGiHP7g.js:2313-2319,2645-2684`: module names and `printTaskErrors` produce `FAIL <relative-file> [ <relative-file> ]` in the current unnamed-project configuration. Identical errors may be grouped: all group headers are emitted before one shared error. Nested case/suite headers have additional titles and are not silently reinterpreted as module-only headers.
- The same chunk at `1882-1891` prints the error's name and message as `Class: message`, not a case-style arrow. The message can contain multiple lines. At `1736-1768`, error preparation can perform work after the headers and before that message is printed.
- `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js:1883-1902`: the logger's error output uses stderr for both headers and details. The case-arrow path remains separately defined by `VerboseReporter` at `index.UpGiHP7g.js:3997-4008`.

These findings support a bounded parser repair, not a product/test/runtime repair. Textual framing is untrusted evidence: it cannot authenticate the reporter against arbitrary unframed user output that imitates it. No exact native error or cause from the preceding diagnostic can be recovered retroactively.

### Proposed ephemeral implementation boundary

Only the previously approved in-memory supervisor/projector strings may be revised after separate approval. No permanent helper, reporter, test file, dependency, runtime source, configuration, Harness artifact, catalog or acceptance metadata is added or changed. Repository documentation may record the scoped result, but cannot mark Q accepted.

1. **Recognize before parsing identity.** Strip the already admitted ANSI framing and use a case-sensitive, anchored native module prefix (`^\s*FAIL(?:\s|$)`, with PowerShell `-cmatch`); anchor the existing failed-case symbol at the start of its frame too. A passed/skipped/todo frame containing `fail` or `FAIL` in its description is not a failure. A genuine failure prefix still invokes the existing stop immediately even when its identity is invalid or unsupported. Unknown/ambiguous failure framing cannot become a successful result.
2. **Preserve termination order and bounds.** Request owned-tree termination at the first recognized failure/unknown, before file/group parsing or optional message association. Keep the **900-second** execution ceiling, existing **5-second** principal-exit wait and **3-second** post-stop drain. Call termination once. Consume only bytes already buffered or arriving through that existing drain; do not wait for a detail line before termination, wait for run-end, extend a bound or add a retry.
3. **Validate the first native module header.** Admit the current explicit form `FAIL <public-test-file> [ <same-public-test-file> ]`. Both operands must normalize to the same captured public relative test path, with containment/identity checks retained; no absolute/UNC/traversal/foreign path is admitted or exposed. The bracket text is never an alternate authority, read target or output field. Unexpected project prefixes, title-bearing headers and other layouts remain unknown rather than broadening the grammar. Recognition must not depend on this stricter identity acceptance.
4. **Use a per-stream association state.** Follow `idle -> module-headers -> first-error-line -> sealed`. Retain the first header's validated identity; never replace an invalid first identity or a missing first detail with a later, more convenient file/error. Permit consecutive valid same-stream native module headers for one shared-error group, validating every member without publishing the list. Attach only that group's first complete `Class: message` line from the same stderr stream. Never correlate stdout/stderr by arrival proximity. Use a fixed `message_scope` value that explicitly says **shared module-group association; first line only**; it does not prove the first file uniquely caused the error. Keep the existing closed nine-field result envelope and 2 KiB total limit.
5. **Seal on ambiguity.** Before the first error line, a blank or unexpected line, console frame, another case, divider/new group, conflicting/malformed header, stack/source snippet, `Serialized Error` or `Caused by` frame makes association unavailable. Do not scan beyond it for a better error. An absent, incomplete, unsupported or unprojectable first line remains unknown or identity-only, never a concrete capture. Later lines of a multiline message are not exposed and cannot be described as captured.
6. **Retain existing privacy projection.** Expose only a finite admitted class/code actually emitted, an inventory-verified public file and a message whose entire first line matches an existing public template. Keep the current strict filesystem code/reason/operation matching with all path operands withheld, numeric timeout template, exact public contract literals and existing redactor; add no arbitrary message/class vocabulary or prefix-plus-suffix fallback. Unrecognized classes, private operands, compared values, stacks, diffs, snippets, URLs, secrets and raw buffers are suppressed, not truncated into output. Keep **64 KiB per stream**, **8 KiB per line**, **2 KiB per record** and existing field bounds. Overflow or decoder/parser/redactor failure yields a fixed unknown result. Destroy raw buffers after projection; save no raw transcript.
7. **Preserve other diagnostic paths.** Keep native case-arrow and standalone-startup projection, process-exit/stream evidence and no-failure completion handling. A complete native success is distinct from an interrupted diagnostic. Principal exit and closed streams still do not prove fixture cleanup or all-writer shutdown. Do not inspect/remove retained roots, normal state or caches.

Delivery remains conditional even after a correct parser: native Vitest emits module headers before preparing/printing the shared error. Immediate termination can win that race. The result must then remain unknown/identity-only; this design does not promise a concrete message or authorize waiting longer, rerunning automatically or attributing a cause without evidence.

### Mandatory pure transcript checks before any further campaign

After separate implementation approval, exercise the **same** decoder, marker recognizer, module association and projector functions intended for the live observer, not a second parser that merely models them. Supply in-memory public source/inventory adapters, fake stop receipts and fixed supervision inputs. The pure checks start no pnpm/Vitest/worker/provider process, create no transcript/fixture/cache file and perform no live filesystem/state operation; preparation may read the public sources already in scope. Use public test identities plus synthetic `C:/synthetic/...` operands/canaries, never retained private paths. Do not load or execute the live supervisor body during these checks.

The bounded synthetic matrix must cover:

- A current native module header plus an admitted explicit `Error: EPERM: operation not permitted, rename ...` line: public identity and code/operation only, both operands withheld.
- Consecutive native group headers plus one shared error: first identity retained, explicit shared first-line scope, no unique-file causal claim; an invalid first header cannot be replaced by the second.
- Equal versus conflicting suffix paths and absolute/UNC/traversal/foreign operands: reject the latter without reading a target outside the public inventory.
- Successful `fail closed` and uppercase `FAIL` descriptions before a true prefix: no stop for the passing frame, exactly one immediate stop at the true failure; verify the stop receipt precedes optional detail parsing.
- Stderr header/detail versus cross-stream detail; known console framing, another case, divider, blank, stack, snippet, `Serialized Error` or `Caused by` before detail: no proximity association or later-error mining. Unframed text imitating native output remains an acknowledged provenance limitation.
- Missing, EOF-incomplete, multiline or unrecognized first error; arbitrary/missing class: unavailable fields stay null/suppressed, with no full-message claim or later-line substitution.
- ANSI, CRLF/LF, chunk boundaries and split valid UTF-8: stable safe framing and projection; malformed or ambiguous decoding remains unknown.
- Each existing stream/line/record cap at and beyond its boundary, private canaries, and internal projection/redactor errors: fixed safe unknown where required, no raw leak, unbounded record or truncated private JSON.
- Existing failed-case/arrow, standalone-startup and complete-success regressions, including cases whose titles contain failure-like words. No candidate plus incomplete execution is not success.

No assertion, guard, timeout or test-selection change in the native suite is included. These are pure observer checks, not profiling, a product test campaign or Q acceptance evidence.

### Approval sequence and stop boundary

1. The next requested approval covers only fresh read-only controls/hashes, implementation/review of the ephemeral capture repair, and the pure transcript checks above. No real `pnpm test`, focused product test, typecheck, validator, original gate, CI, audit or browser invocation is included.
2. Stop at the first failed/unknown pure check, exposing only safe public diagnostics. Do not loosen assertions, grammar/privacy rules, guardrails or limits to make it pass. If all pure checks pass, report their scope and stop anyway; their result does not prove delivery from a real interrupted suite.
3. A real native verbose diagnostic would require a **new, separate human approval** after reviewing that result, with its original 900-second ceiling and first-failure/unknown stop intact. No retry/campaign is preauthorized by this design or its implementation approval.

The execution-safety and file-state skills constrain the repair to observation, unchanged termination/authority and public synthetic inputs. No code/parser implementation, pure check, test/probe/campaign, profiling, cleanup, commit, push or merge occurred while preparing this proposal.

Design-only closure: independent native-format and safety/privacy reviews found no blocker. Synthetic transcripts must retain native framing, including blanks; an unavailable association is not repaired by deleting framing. The 1229-path inventory changed only this document, with 1228 other paths unchanged and none removed. Static whitespace inspection was clean; branch/HEAD and the empty index were preserved. No execution or acceptance check was performed.

## T012 bounded rename-EPERM investigation design - 2026-10-17; proposed

The owner requested a bounded investigation design, with no guard/timeout change or further campaign. This turn is **design only**: source inspection and this documentary proposal, not observer implementation, synthetic checks, filesystem probes or native execution. The sole repository edit is this design document. T012/Q remains pending and no approval/waiver metadata changes.

### Evidence and competing explanations

Known runtime evidence is the first admitted `Error / EPERM / rename` line associated with the group whose first public module is `cli/surtec-state.test.ts`, observed at 44.22 seconds. No case title, operands, stack or complete module-group membership was retained. The paths were deliberately withheld and discarded: they cannot be recovered by inspecting the safe record. Neither permissions, contention, an antivirus handle, a specific cache nor a product writer is established. The interrupted principal's exit -1 is not a completed native-suite result.

Read-only inspection provides bounded candidates, not a diagnosis:

- `cli/surtec-state.test.ts:1-15,50-76` imports a substantial application graph; its explicit `surtec-cli-` temporary fixture creation and state writes live in hooks/tests, not an unconditional top-level seed. `cli/surtec-state.ts` gates `main()` on direct invocation. This does not prove every transitive import is side-effect-free or that fixture hooks ran before the module error.
- `lib/state/store.ts:24-40` replaces JSON using `<target>.tmp -> <target>` under existing maintenance guards. `lib/state/workflows.ts:60-79` uses a unique staging file and wraps replacement errors as `WorkflowStateError`. These are real application writers reached by some fixtures, not permission to change their locking or error handling.
- Installed Vitest 4.1.11, `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js:695-701,838-848,1010-1030,1138-1153`, routes temporary module copies through `saveCachedModule -> atomicWriteFile -> writeFile/rename`. **Temporary copies can use this path even with persistent `experimental.fsModuleCache` disabled.** Staging names have `.tmp-<time>-<random>` form; module-copy destinations use SHA-1-shaped leaves beneath the generated project temporary directory and environment folder. Temporary roots are constructed from `tmpdir()` and generated identities (`10707,13087`); none was enumerated or inspected here.
- The same installed source at `971,1070-1072,9328` keeps persistent filesystem-module caching off by default; `vitest.config.ts` does not enable it. Its separate public default under `node_modules/.experimental-vitest-cache` must not be conflated with ordinary temporary module copies or declared active from function presence.
- Installed Vite, `node_modules/vite/dist/node/chunks/dep-Dm0c1Wj2.js:14930-14960,15553-15569`, also renames dependency-cache directories, using its existing Windows-safe rename path. That dependency implementation and its retry/timeout behavior remain untouched.
- Native `printTaskErrors` groups identical errors before printing a shared detail (`index.UpGiHP7g.js:2645-2684`). The first group's file remains a correlation identity, not a unique causal owner.

The useful next observation is therefore **where the two operands belong and whether their staging relationship matches a public writer shape**, not a raw path/stack dump or a speculative fixture/cache repair.

### Proposed observer-only delta after separate approval

Change only the ephemeral JavaScript projector's handling of an already admitted module `EPERM` + `rename` line and its pure synthetic harness. The existing full-match filesystem parser already holds both operand strings briefly before withholding them. Do not change PowerShell stream/group recognition, immediate termination, test command, worker/pool, native stderr/stdout, environment, loader, permissions, tests or product runtime. No permanent helper/reporter, dependency patch or Harness/catalog edit is proposed.

1. Add a small pure lexical classifier over those existing two operands. Pass repository root and the original sanitized temporary-root context through explicit observer-owned adapters; hold their actual values only in memory. Preparation may obtain the existing `TEMP/TMP` values and observer `tmpdir()` without exporting or overriding them. No directory walk, stat/read, realpath, file opening, path probing, handle/process inventory or normal-state read is performed by classification.
2. Use closed operand-location enums: `repository-vite-cache`, `repository-vitest-persistent-cache`, `cli-fixture-state-shape`, `temporary-other`, `repository-other`, `outside-known-roots`, `unknown`. Recognize repository cache locations only by the public default segments reviewed above; a custom/unverified location stays generic/unknown. Recognize the CLI fixture shape only beneath an admitted temporary root with the exact public `surtec-cli-` family and `state` structure. No raw basename, project/user name, nonce, hash, timestamp, depth count or path fragment is emitted.
3. Independently report a closed staging-shape enum: `vitest-module-temp-shape` for same-parent `.tmp-<numeric>-<bounded-random>` to SHA-1-shaped destination beneath an admitted temporary root with the reviewed project/environment layout; `json-sibling-staging-shape` only for `<target>.tmp` or `<target>.<UUID>.tmp` to that same JSON target in the same parent; otherwise `other`/`unknown`. Add only `same-parent`, `different-parent` or `unknown` relationship evidence. These are **textual shapes, not authenticated ownership, existence, directory type, write authority or proof of a lock/ACL cause**. A look-alike path can share the same shape.
4. Reject ambiguous lexical inputs before classification: relative/drive-relative paths, UNC/device/extended forms, traversal or dot segments, ADS/extra colons, controls/NUL, URLs/encoded separators and Windows trailing-dot/space aliases. Normalize admitted drive/slash/case forms with component-boundary containment, not substring prefix matching. Ambiguous roots/overlaps and missing context yield `unknown`, not a more convenient bucket. Root context is not inferred from private normal state or a later error/group.
5. Keep the current **nine string/null fields**, original safe `message`, explicit native class/code and existing module-group first-line scope. Append only a fixed-format sequence of admitted location/shape/relationship enums to `message_scope`. Never interpolate raw operands, exception text, context roots or dynamic property names. Preserve 64 KiB/stream, 8 KiB/line, 1024-byte fields and 2 KiB/record, existing redactor and fixed unknown fallback. Do not increase any bound to fit new metadata. Unsupported messages still use the existing unavailable/unknown behavior; non-target diagnostic paths are unchanged.
6. Preserve the **900-second** execution ceiling, **5-second** principal-exit wait and **3-second** drain, requesting termination at the first failure/unknown before classification. Observe only the same first candidate and already buffered/drained data; no waiting for a stack, new trace hook, profiler, debug environment, monkey patch, injected loader, retry, second error or later group. Clear operand buffers after projection and persist only safe enums. Classification failure cannot grant execution or trigger an automatic follow-up.

### Pure verification and separate execution decisions

The next proposed approval is for ephemeral implementation and **pure synthetic checks only**, then STOP. Reuse the exact classifier/projector functions with injected fictional roots and public source views; do not model them in a second implementation. Preserve the 69 existing projector checks and add targeted cases for:

- Ordinary Vitest temporary-module staging even while persistent caching is off; persistent repository cache, Vite-cache and CLI-fixture-state shapes as separate observations. Include the observed public first-module identity `cli/surtec-state.test.ts` with a synthetic module-cache shape and another public module with a synthetic state shape: never derive operand ownership from the module filename.
- Same/different parents, mismatched target/hash/staging layout, a look-alike outside the admitted roots and generic repository/temporary/external locations. Do not turn a lexical match into a causal conclusion.
- Case/slash variants with strict component boundaries; similar-prefix sibling roots; missing/overlapping context; relative/drive-relative, traversal, UNC/device/extended, ADS, trailing-dot/space and encoded/URL/control inputs.
- Both private operands and private context canaries absent from all output; no filename/hash/nonce/timestamp fragments; unchanged native message/class/code and first-group identity. Unknown operand context must be explicit, not suppressed into a claimed root cause.
- Existing first-invalid/no-later-mining and cross-stream regressions; classifier/adapter/redactor exceptions; exact/over field and whole-record bounds after appended enums; no fallback raw dump or truncated private JSON.

All checks use memory and fake adapters: no real rename, filesystem probe, cache or fixture creation, product import/worker/provider, pnpm/Vitest process or normal-state access. The unchanged 46 PowerShell checks need not be replayed because their code is outside this delta. Any failed/unknown check stops immediately without repair/retry; even a complete synthetic PASS stops before native execution.

Only **after another explicit human approval**, one full original `pnpm test --reporter=verbose` diagnostic could observe the added enums under the unchanged 900-second/fail-first controls. This design does not authorize it. If the first failure differs, details are missing, or classification is generic/unknown, report that result and stop rather than filtering/repeating until this EPERM appears. A cache-shaped result can support a scoped investigation of that writer; a state-shaped result can support a scoped state-writer investigation. Neither authorizes deleting a cache, changing TEMP/TMP, disabling caching, serializing workers, modifying ACL/security software, relaxing maintenance/ownership guards, increasing retries/timeouts or implementing a product repair. Cleanup remains unconfirmed unless separately evidenced.

No campaign, synthetic/parser check, typecheck, validator, CI, audit, browser smoke, profiler, observer implementation, normal-state/cache/retained-root inspection or cleanup ran in this design turn. ## T012 temporary-module publication investigation design - 2026-10-17; proposed

### Decision boundary and known evidence

The owner requested a bounded design, not another campaign, guard/timeout change or repair. This turn performs public installed-source inspection and edits only this design document. No observer implementation, synthetic checks, filesystem probe or native test run is authorized or performed. The fresh 1229-path source inventory matches the preceding diagnostic closure; branch/HEAD and the empty index are unchanged. T012 stays unchecked, Q unaccepted and the waiver null.

The last diagnostic's concrete EPERM/rename line has `temporary-other` source/destination, `vitest-module-temp-shape` and `same-parent`, associated with the shared group whose first public identity is `cli/surtec-state.test.ts`. Its 53.651-second interrupted result does not identify a unique test, actual writer, missing permission, held handle or security-software cause. Lexical categories do not authenticate an on-disk Vitest file; redacted operands cannot be recovered or inspected retrospectively. Prior cleanup remains unconfirmed.

### Installed-source findings

Read-only inspection of installed Vitest **4.1.11**, `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js`, provides the following public implementation facts:

- Lines 23 and 838-848 bind `writeFile`, `rename`, `stat` and `unlink` from `node:fs/promises`. `atomicWriteFile` writes a same-parent `.tmp-<time>-<random>` file, awaits rename to the destination and then attempts staging cleanup in a `finally`; cleanup errors are swallowed. It contains no rename retry or destination unlink fallback. A reported rename error is not evidence that its finally cleanup succeeded.
- Lines 691-701 call that helper from `saveCachedModule`. Lines 1010-1029 also reach it for temporary module copies when persistent caching is disabled and temporary copies are requested; the destination is a SHA-1-shaped module-ID leaf under project temporary directory/environment. Lines 1070-1071 return no persistent cache path when that feature is off. Disabling persistent caching again would not establish removal of this temporary-copy path.
- Lines 961 and 1139-1149 deduplicate currently pending saves by cache-path string in a module-local map, deleting the entry when its promise settles. Lines 1017-1028 reuse a temporary path saved on the transform-result object. These mechanisms are not an OS lock, a cross-process guarantee or proof that later saves never replace an existing target; no observed race is inferred from their presence.
- Lines 2732-2747 and 2907 pass the worker's `cacheFs` flag to module fetching; the `ForksPoolWorker` at 3124-3126 advertises `cacheFs = true`. This explains an installed path to temporary copies without changing the selected pool; it is not fresh evidence of the failed run's complete resolved configuration.
- Lines 10703-10707 and 13087 allocate per-instance temporary-directory names beneath `tmpdir()`. Project close at 10970-10981 includes temporary-directory removal, whose errors are swallowed at 11059-11062. This is lifecycle implementation, not evidence that close ran or raced with the failed rename. Do not inspect or delete retained roots to test that conjecture.

The reviewed chunk SHA-256 is **`A236001D048380E2C67D05423FC9EA3F26B07EE019BA8D6E622082F29D49102E`**. The uniquely matched `atomicWriteFile(realFilePath, data)` function, normalized from CRLF to LF only and excluding a following line ending, is **358 characters**, SHA-256 **`6F43C47DF8D7F23032A41C921066D1D1C22E91B0D3FEB1A59C677798E5161B14`**. These pins bind a future ephemeral probe to this reviewed implementation, not a dependency update, Harness pin change or trust assertion about arbitrary installed code.

### Smallest next investigation, after approval

Build an **ephemeral, file-free Node probe and pure synthetic harness**, not another full-suite observer or permanent project command. Reuse the exact uniquely extracted/pinned helper source in a narrow function factory with explicit `join`/`dirname` and filesystem adapters; do not import or evaluate the entire Vitest CLI chunk, a test file or the application's dependency graph. Reject version/chunk/helper mismatch before any execution or fixture creation; do not silently regenerate pins or substitute a handwritten imitation. Real path functions must use the reviewed dependency semantics. No global monkey patch, injected native-test loader, instrumentation, trace, profiler or new dependency is proposed.

The first approval requested below covers implementation and **pure checks only**. If those checks pass, STOP. A second explicit authorization would permit exactly one local filesystem-probe process, not Vitest/pnpm, with the original installed Node, the same sanitized allowlist/CI/TEMP/TMP and no elevated permissions or sandbox change. A live probe must use the existing 900-second outer ceiling, 5-second principal-exit wait and 3-second post-stop drain; there is no native Vitest timeout to override because no test runner starts. Session handles must be retained and resumed, never interpreted as unknown merely because a response is still pending.

That separately authorized live probe would execute at most these **three sequential rename attempts**, advancing only after the preceding stage and its content assertion succeed:

1. **`control-new`**: create/close a tiny fictional source and rename it to a previously absent sibling with Node's ordinary filesystem promise API. Verify exactly the expected bytes at the destination. This establishes only the baseline operation in the newly owned fixture.
2. **`helper-new`**: call the exact pinned helper with a small fictional module string and an absent SHA-1-shaped target beneath an owned project/environment-shaped subtree. Verify exact target bytes. The target is a fixed synthetic identity, never a hash derived from private project content.
3. **`helper-replace`**: call that same helper once with different fictional bytes and the now-existing, closed target. Verify replacement bytes. This distinguishes first publication from closed-target replacement in this controlled fixture; it is not a simulation of open-handle or concurrent-worker contention.

No loop, stress run, deliberate lock, open-destination experiment, parallel writer, retry, delay, cache reset or full-suite follow-up is included. A failed or unknown stage stops progression immediately, aside from eligible owned cleanup and safe result projection. A successful probe is not a repaired project or a gate PASS.

### Scope, evidence and cleanup controls

- A live probe may allocate only one unique `mkdtemp` root under the original `tmpdir()`, using an explicit probe-owned prefix; it must not change TEMP/TMP, use the repository cache or open a historical Vitest/CLI/normal-state root. Keep the root/nonce only in memory. No directory enumeration, process/handle inventory, ACL/security inspection or broad filesystem search is included.
- Before any write/rename/unlink/removal, admit exact absolute targets beneath the canonical newly owned root, reject symlink/reparse/containment ambiguity, and check a per-invocation in-memory path/effect ledger. The helper's staging destination is admitted and recorded before its filesystem adapter performs the effect. Use at most eight owned files, four owned directories beneath the allocated root and 1024 UTF-8 bytes per payload; exceeding a bound fails closed. These controls protect the probe's scope, not arbitrary same-user filesystem races.
- Only Node builtins, reviewed path helpers and the narrow pinned function may run. No product import, agent/worker/provider/model, authentication, network, sibling mutation, package install, dependency patch, Vitest configuration, Harness/catalog/spec approval or product state modification is allowed.
- Emit a closed safe result: outcome/stage, operation enum, allowlisted filesystem error code or `unknown`, independently tracked setup/content/cleanup status, principal exit and termination facts. Do not emit `error.message`, stack, path, basename, environment, payload, nonce, module-ID hash or raw stdout/stderr. Retain the existing strict UTF-8 and 64-KiB stream/8-KiB line/1024-byte field/2-KiB record caps; unexpected output or projection uncertainty stops as unknown, never as a raw fallback.
- Preserve the first diagnostic failure independently of later cleanup results. The helper's expected `stat` ENOENT after successful rename is normal caught cleanup behavior, not a new failed diagnostic. An unexpected cleanup failure must still be reported even if the helper suppresses it. A cleanup PASS requires positive scoped evidence, never just a helper return or swallowed exception.
- Close owned handles before cleanup. Remove only exact admitted owned files, then empty directories using nonrecursive removal after rechecking ownership/containment. No recursive delete, scan, guessed path, retry or force option is included. If the process/handles/ownership are unknown, retain the fixture and report cleanup unconfirmed; termination never proves successful cleanup. This cannot clean up any older run.

### Required pure checks and interpretation

Use the same helper/factory, probe state machine, scope/effect ledger, safe projector and orchestration result handling proposed for live use, with fake filesystem/process adapters and fictional paths only. The pure check matrix must cover:

- Unchanged source extraction/hash admission; mismatch, duplicate/missing helper and initialization failures before any effect; no whole-chunk evaluation or live filesystem adapter execution.
- Exact write/rename/finally order; new-target and replacement success/content checks; injected write/rename/verification errors stop later stages; existing destination is never unlinked to make rename succeed; no retries or deliberate delays.
- Expected staging-stat ENOENT, leftover staging unlink, swallowed helper-cleanup error, primary-error preservation and cleanup failure/unknown outcomes; no false cleanup PASS.
- Exact root/target admission, outside/sibling-prefix/traversal/symlink/reparse ambiguity, operation/file/directory/payload bounds, no-effect refusal, nonrecursive cleanup order and no cleanup when ownership/handle shutdown is unknown.
- Pending-session continuation followed by terminal zero/nonzero; missing handle/result, timeout, malformed/extra output and process termination uncertainty; no second child launch or automatic rerun.
- Private path/environment/nonce/payload/exception canaries, fixed unknown fallback, allowed-code validation and all retained byte/field/record limits. Expected injected failures are assertions, not ignored real execution uncertainty.

Run these checks only through an in-memory Node/stdin harness, without pnpm/Vitest, real rename, fixture creation, product/runtime import or another campaign. Stop at the first unexpected failure or unknown, with no repair/retry. No existing 46/69/124-check observer matrix needs replay because that observer is outside this delta. No typecheck/schema/build/CI/browser/profiling or paid smoke is included in this ephemeral JavaScript-only slice.

Interpret a separately authorized real probe conservatively: control failure narrows evidence to the basic operation in a fresh owned temporary fixture; helper-new failure after control success narrows it to that publication sequence/layout; helper-replace failure after both prior successes distinguishes existing-target replacement in the fixture. None alone proves ACLs, antivirus, sandbox policy or the original suite's cause. If all three pass, basic closed-file publication is not reproduced here; concurrent module loading, lifecycle conditions and external interference remain open, not disproved. Stop and design the next bounded decision rather than automatically launching a campaign, serializing workers or bypassing a failing rename.

### Approval and delivery

**Proposed next authorization: implement only the ephemeral helper/probe/harness and run its pure synthetic checks, then STOP even on PASS.** Persist only bounded English evidence in this design and the Q runbook; no permanent implementation file is proposed. A live three-stage probe needs separate explicit approval after that result. Any subsequent project repair or original Q gate/full suite also remains a separate decision. Approval of this design is not permission to change guards, timeouts, worker counts/pool, TEMP/TMP, persistent caching, ACLs/security software, dependencies, publication or deferred Phase P functionality.

This design turn executed only source reads/searches, static hashes and source-integrity inventory; no implementation, synthetic check, real filesystem probe, fixture/cache/normal-state mutation, native campaign, gate, CI, commit, push or merge occurred. ## T012 probe initialization error-capture design - 2026-10-17; proposed

### Current authority and evidence gap

The owner requested this bounded design only, without repeating checks, real filesystem probes or guard/timeout changes. This turn reads instructions, relevant prepared code and public documentation and edits only this design document. It does not implement or execute a replacement initializer/projector, resolve/import a dependency through Node, run assertions or allocate a fixture. Fresh integrity covers the same 1229 paths with no drift; branch/HEAD and the empty index are unchanged. T012/Q remains pending and no approval/waiver metadata changes.

The prior 51338-character harness returned the outer `source-or-harness-preparation` fallback, exit 1, without a retained exception/substep or check count. Its in-memory boot performs, in order: `createRequire` using the existing Vitest package-manifest path, `require.resolve("pathe")`, conversion of the resolved entry to a file URL, dynamic import, version-file read/JSON parse and CLI-chunk read. The final `catch` has no error binding. The stdin driver relays the fixed safe JSON and discards stderr. Static inspection confirms the information-loss path; it does not determine which operation threw, recover discarded text or prove a module-resolution, permission or pin failure. Vitest's manifest declaring `pathe` is not proof that this particular resolution/import succeeded.

### Minimal ephemeral delta

Prepare an **initialization-only** derivative from the exact existing boot, with a separate small safe error projector. Keep the original root, manifest/chunk filenames, `createRequire` argument, dependency identifier, URL conversion, import mechanism and read/parse order. Split compound expressions only to set a fixed stage immediately before each existing operation. Do not correct resolution, canonicalize its base, add search paths/fallbacks, reinstall dependencies or run the original harness in order to discover the error.

Use the closed stage set: `bootstrap`, `create-require`, `resolve-pathe`, `path-helper-url`, `import-pathe`, `read-version-manifest`, `parse-version-manifest`, `read-cli-chunk`, `initialization-completed`. Preserve public source labels independently from private values: `path-helper`, `version-manifest`, `cli-chunk` or null. Stage/label values come from observer-owned constants, never from exception text. Retain the currently resolved entry and source filenames only in memory for exact comparisons, not as output.

After the same boot reaches `sourceInput`, emit `initialization-completed-no-checks` and exit normally **before loading or calling any probe factory, scope adapter, fake filesystem, assertion matrix, `check`, `runPublicationProbe`, `admitHelperSource` or stage operation**. Even the original first source-pin assertion must not run. Do not concatenate the 51338-character harness or attach a success continuation. This slice isolates boot behavior; a successful initializer cannot establish the rest of that harness, its synthetic matrix or the real probe as valid.

On the first caught initialization exception, project it once and exit nonzero immediately. Do not retry, probe a second resolver, attempt recovery, collect later errors or continue to reads/checks after the failing operation. Static imports of Node builtins remain unchanged. A parse/startup error outside the catch remains a bounded driver/initialization unknown, not a license to dump stderr or repeat the invocation.

### Exact but private-safe error evidence

The capture must identify the **observed stage, admitted native exception class/code and, when safely recognizable, its complete first message line**, not promise an unredacted exception/stack dump. Use a small closed record containing `initialization_capture`, `stage`, `source_label`, `error_class`, `error_code`, `message`, `message_scope`, `reason`, `checks_started`, `real_probe_started`, `native_suite_started` and `files_written`. The four effect flags are always false for this slice. No alternate schema may carry hidden raw details.

- Admit native classes `Error`, `TypeError`, `SyntaxError`, `RangeError` and `ReferenceError`, otherwise use explicit unknown/null. Read only admitted data properties without invoking custom getters/toString or serializing the exception. Projection/property-access failure must use a fixed unknown record, never another exception's text.
- Admit codes `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED`, `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_DIR_IMPORT`, `ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE`, `ERR_INVALID_ARG_VALUE`, `ERR_INVALID_URL`, `ERR_UNKNOWN_FILE_EXTENSION`, `ENOENT`, `EACCES`, `EPERM`, `ENOTDIR` and `EISDIR`; retain a native absent code as null and an unrecognized code as unknown. A code is recorded from the exception, not inferred from its stage or chosen to fit a hypothesis.
- Admit a literal first line `Cannot find module 'pathe'` only with its consistent native missing-module code and `resolve-pathe` stage; its later require-stack suffix is withheld. Likewise admit the public literal missing-package form only if it actually appears with the consistent code/stage. Never replace a different module name with `pathe` or turn a generic import failure into proof that this entry is missing.
- For a recognized filesystem error line, require code/reason/operation consistency and exact in-memory operand agreement with the version-manifest, CLI-chunk or resolved-entry input at that stage. Emit only its observed code/operation and the fixed public source label with `[path withheld]`. - `message_scope` must say first-line-only with private suffix/stack withheld. No path, stack, require stack, resolved filename/URL, username, environment value, nonce, payload, credential or raw transcript is persisted or emitted. After separate approval and successful new pure capture checks, run exactly one structural invocation through the same command-scoped permitted host route used by the previous attempt:


**Neither `-Run` nor `-ConfirmExecution` is supplied.** Admit this immutable argument vector before launch; do not forward arbitrary arguments, invoke private acceptance helpers directly or alter/import an executable copy of the validator to intercept execution. This makes the no-suite boundary independent of whether the early error reproduces. The existing `-TimeoutSeconds 900` value is retained but starts no acceptance checks in structural mode. Normal host review is required; no persistent grants, environment/module-path repair, alternate host or retry after refusal is included.

Reuse the reviewed secret-redaction rules and explicit path suppression from the existing capture approach: secret assignments/token families/credential URLs must be redacted; repository, home, temp, dependency/module roots and other absolute drive/UNC/POSIX paths must not escape. `lib/security/redaction.ts` alone does not remove filesystem paths and is not sufficient by itself. ## T012 bounded incremental CI capture design - 2026-10-17; proposed, not implemented

### Evidence and exact scope

The owner requested a narrow design only: prevent ordinary CI output from exhausting retained memory without increasing limits, changing guards/timeouts or repeating CI/the approved gate. Static inspection of the qualified ephemeral supervisor (`EA425DC5C5146764072B3FE7A2F91CC8EEB8BA7D19CF8F6531C5437BF823703D`) confirms that each read appends to `held[stream]`, checks 65536 bytes and 8192 bytes per line, then retains every completed line until process exit. This explains how cumulative bounded lines can exhaust retention; the observed `stream-boundary` does not identify the stream or native test result.

Implementation, if separately approved, is limited to this memory-only CI capturer and its pure synthetic harness. Repository changes remain evidence in this design and `docs/runbooks/phase-q-acceptance.md` only. No new repository script/module, profiler, reporter, log file, disk spool, ring buffer, dependency, Harness validator/pin, CI command/filter or product runtime change is proposed.

### Consume completed lines, preserve guards

1. Keep separate stdout/stderr readers and the existing 1024-character read arrays. Append a completed read to that stream's pending text. Execute the existing retained-stream UTF-8 check and every-line UTF-8 check **before** parsing, sanitizing, dropping or normalizing any bytes. Their predicates, thresholds and fail-closed reasons remain unchanged; do not truncate oversized lines to make them pass.
2. Process each complete LF-delimited line once, then remove the consumed prefix immediately. Keep only the unfinished suffix for the next read. CRLF can span reads; a bare CR is not a new delimiter that bypasses the existing line guard. Preserve strict decoding, split-surrogate handling and original raw-byte accounting before ANSI SGR removal. No raw-history collection, replay buffer or deferred output queue may grow with run length.
3. Replace end-of-process whole-stdout regex scans with constant-size reducers using the same anchored public numeric summary patterns. Store one parsed value per existing total and a saturating absent/unique/duplicate marker, not a list of matches. Missing, duplicated, invalid or overflowing totals remain null; they do not become zero or evidence of completed checks. Recognize a valid numeric final fragment at EOF as the old whole-string matching could; an incomplete diagnostic is never called complete.
4. For optional failure diagnostics, pass a complete recognized stderr line through the existing redaction/projection rules; retain at most the first complete sanitized diagnostic, within the existing 1024-byte message and 2048-byte terminal-record ceilings. Do not persist raw stderr, private paths, test names, assertion bodies or unrecognized text. Wrapped, partial or unprojectable diagnostics remain explicitly unavailable/unknown instead of being represented as a complete message. Diagnostic extraction stays optional and cannot invent a native failure or erase a nonzero exit. No arbitrary multiline-history reconstruction is proposed.
5. A completed native process with both streams closed retains its real exit code as the result: exit 0 passes CI, nonzero fails CI regardless of numeric summary text. Numeric totals or success-looking lines never confer execution success. A supervision/encoding/read/line/record error remains unknown with the qualified first-trigger reason captured before cleanup. Preserve the legacy startup field's documented limitation; correcting unrelated metadata is outside this slice.
6. Clear residual and derived capture state in the existing finalization path. The existing 900-second ceiling, 5-second exit wait, 3-second drain, Kill(true), pending-read handling, principal/stream completion conditions, fixed native command and sanitized environment/PATHEXT remain unchanged. No extra process or filesystem adapter is added.

The **64-KiB ceiling remains a retained-memory bound**, checked before every reduction, not a newly raised value. The live raw suffix is constrained by the existing 8-KiB line guard; one 1024-character read plus fixed numeric state and one bounded projected record also remain within the original retained-memory ceiling. This changes the buffer lifecycle, not the guard predicates. It deliberately permits total consumed output over the lifetime of a command to exceed retained memory without keeping it. It does **not** claim to preserve a separate 64-KiB lifetime-emission quota: preserving such a cumulative quota while consuming more than 64 KiB would be contradictory. Approval of this design must cover this explicit consume-and-release distinction; there is no hidden output-limit increase or silent tail truncation.

### Synthetic qualification only after approval

Prepare source in memory with integrity hash and static syntax validation; execute only the pure reducer and extracted projection/guard fragments. Do not invoke the real supervisor or a fake adapter that launches subprocesses or accesses filesystem state. Stop at the first failed/unknown check without an automatic fix/retry.

- Feed more than 64 KiB of ordinary bounded lines to each stream incrementally, without materializing a full transcript. Assert retained raw/derived state stays bounded, processed lines are released and late numeric summaries still survive. Verify no per-line/match/error list grows with input.
- Cover alternating streams, empty streams, one-character/read-boundary splits, CRLF, EOF without LF, split ANSI SGR sequences, split supplementary Unicode and malformed/incomplete encoding. Apply raw-byte guards before transformations; do not mistake characters for bytes.
- Prove the existing 8192/8193-byte line, 65536/65537-byte retained-stream, 1024/1025-byte projected-message and 2048/2049-byte terminal-record boundaries still pass/refuse as applicable. Exercise stream/record guards directly with synthetic boundary state even if ordinary line consumption keeps live retained state far below those ceilings.
- Cover unique/missing/duplicate/invalid/overflowing totals, success-looking output followed by native exit 1, absent totals with native exit 0, unread/unfinished streams and diagnostic projection failures. No summary or missing text may override native status or fabricate acceptance/cleanup.
- Place fake private/credential/path canaries early, late and across read boundaries; assert no raw text/history escapes into terminal records. Prove first-trigger reasons survive simulated cleanup errors and that uncertainty remains uncertainty.
- Compare the source delta against the qualified supervisor: only capture state, consume/reduce helpers, their insertion after unchanged guards, final numeric/diagnostic projection and capture-state clearing may differ. Assert native arguments, environment, read sizes, original guard expressions, clocks, start/wait/kill/drain/completion paths and reason helper are unchanged. All live-effect counters must stay zero.

Synthetic PASS would qualify the capture only. A real CI, audit and both smokes require separate execution authorization; the successful original four-check gate must not be repeated automatically. A nonzero native exit or another supervision reason remains a real stop, not a waived failure or a guarantee this change closes Q.

### Current design-only delivery boundary

Only read-only inspection, baseline hashing and documentary edits/whitespace review belong to this turn. No capture implementation, synthetic checks, source parsing campaign, CI, gate, suite/typecheck, audit, browser or filesystem probe was executed. The execution-safety skill constrained privacy, memory bounds and native-result authority. Baseline: **1229 unchanged source paths**, same Q branch/HEAD and empty index. Only this design and the runbook record the proposal; no normal-state/sibling operation, uncertain-root cleanup, paid call, commit, push or merge. **T012/Q remains pending without waiver; the original four-check gate remains PASS.** Next decision: approve this ephemeral implementation plus synthetic-only qualification, not native acceptance.

## T012 bounded CI-stage observation design - 2026-10-17; proposed, not implemented

### Question and scope

The owner requested a design only to identify the stage observed when the existing 900-second whole-CI ceiling expires, without changing limits/guards/timeouts or repeating CI/the approved gate. Latest native evidence is only `ci: unknown`, `reason: outer-timeout`, termination requested and principal exit confirmed; no active stage or completed-check inventory survived. This design cannot recover that discarded history or attribute the previous timeout retrospectively.

Extend only the qualified memory-only capturer (`6083C38FFC4ECD0EE47470AFFC7F0D876A386ACAB872F4CC9D2EC0402DF1E53A`) and its pure synthetic matrix, if separately approved. Keep repository edits documentary in this design and `docs/runbooks/phase-q-acceptance.md`. No package/lockfile, CI script, product/Harness code, validator/pin, reporter/plugin, disk log, subprocess inventory, per-test tracing or generic profiler is proposed. Preserve one unmodified `pnpm ci:check`, including its `&&` chain: do not execute stages separately or give each one a new 900-second allowance.

### Fixed stage vocabulary from the current package manifest

| Order / public stage | Expected script command; observation only |
| --- | --- |
| 1 / `repository:check` | `tsx scripts/check-repository-consistency.ts` |
| 2 / `harness:check` | `pwsh -NoProfile -File .agents/scripts/check-harness.ps1` |
| 3 / `schemas:check` | `node scripts/check-schemas.mjs` |
| 4 / `secrets:check` | `node scripts/check-secrets.mjs` |
| 5 / `evals:check` | `tsx scripts/run-evals.ts && tsx scripts/run-outcome-evals.ts` |
| 6 / `test` | `vitest run` |
| 7 / `typecheck` | `tsc --noEmit` |
| 8 / `build` | `vite build` |

These eight names and expected commands come from the inspected `surtec-control-plane@0.1.0` manifest. They are not replacement commands to execute. The initial `ci:check` wrapper announcement is not a ninth stage; no eval substage/test-name inference is added. Manifest drift or different/unrecognized package-manager rendering must produce unavailable/ambiguous attribution, not a guessed mapping or a command change.

### Passive recognition and bounded state

Observe only complete stdout lines already admitted by the unchanged raw UTF-8 stream/line guards. Pair an exact allowlisted package/script announcement with its corresponding exact command announcement on the next nonempty stdout line; normalize only existing ANSI SGR rendering and CRLF boundaries after raw guards. Match the public package/version/stage prefix and expected command, but never retain or emit the announcement's working-directory suffix, whole header or command text. A partial EOF header, stderr lookalike, bare substring such as a test title, foreign package/version, unmatched command, duplicate or out-of-order stage cannot establish a new stage. Unsupported rendering must remain an observation gap. The parser never suppresses output validation or native failures.

Use a **separate fixed observer object**, not an unbounded line/event list or a changed numeric-summary accumulator: eight preallocated stage slots, an optional pending stage enum, a monotonic index and fixed availability/reason fields. Store at most one integer start-observation timestamp per stage. Take timestamps from the existing supervisor stopwatch when an admitted complete announcement pair is consumed; there is no new timer, wait, sampling loop or reset. The existing capturer still discards consumed text immediately. Stage identifiers and numeric snapshots must remain independent of mutable scratch variables such as `$line`.

Advance only in the reviewed eight-stage order. Missing stages remain missing; an incomplete pair makes current attribution unavailable, and duplicate/out-of-order/unmatched recognized announcements make it ambiguous without silently reconstructing the gap. Retain earlier observed slots as partial observations, not completed checks. Ordinary unrelated log lines are consumed without expanding state. Observation uncertainty does not change native execution, trigger a new early abort, convert native failure to PASS or replace the existing supervision reason.

### Terminal evidence and honest interpretation

Add one compact `stage_observation` field to the existing terminal projection, containing only availability, a fixed reason, last fully paired stage enum (or null), pending stage enum (or null), total observed CI elapsed milliseconds, elapsed milliseconds since the last paired announcement when attribution is available, and up to eight `{stage, observed_at_ms}` entries. Use fixed enums and nonnegative finite integer timestamps; missing initialization or invalid/nonmonotonic injected clock values become unavailable metadata, never invented zero timings. Validate worst-case serialization synthetically within the unchanged 1024-byte string-field / 2048-byte whole-record bounds, including existing diagnostic/fallback fields; no extra output channel or raised cap is permitted.

Capture the original supervision reason first, as today. Freeze an immutable observation snapshot **immediately afterward and before termination/drain**; final native completion also takes a snapshot without changing status. Drained bytes cannot advance the frozen snapshot, and cleanup errors cannot overwrite its first reason or add cleanup time to the stage interval. Clear mutable observer state with the existing capture finalization. Preserve the documented limitations of the inherited startup flag; do not infer Vitest start from top-level process start.

These timestamps measure **parent-observed announcement intervals**, not exact child start/finish times, CPU time, per-stage native exit codes or a trusted process inventory. Child stdout remains untrusted and could imitate a complete announcement pair; paired known markers reduce accidental matches but do not authenticate origin. Consequently label the result **last observed CI stage**, not an independently proven active command. The next banner is not a receipt that the previous check passed, and a timeout in the last observed interval is not proof a named test caused it. If attribution is missing/ambiguous, state that gap explicitly. Native CI exit and principal/stream completion remain the only existing execution-result authority; observations cannot grant acceptance or cleanup proof.

### Invariants and synthetic acceptance before native execution

Keep the 900-second whole-command predicate and stopwatch origin, 65536-byte retained-stream / 8192-byte raw-line / 1024-byte message / 2048-byte record limits, 1024-character reads, 5-second exit wait, 3-second drain, Kill(true), pending-read loop, completion conditions, fixed native arguments, sanitized environment/PATHEXT, summary/privacy logic and first-reason capture unchanged. Stage metadata must fit inside the existing retained-payload budget; it is not another memory allowance. No product state, sibling, paid runtime or browser effect is needed.

If approved, implement only the in-memory observer/capture integration and extend pure synthetic qualification. Preserve all existing 112 checks and their meanings; add cases for all eight ordered marker pairs, no/partial/wrong/duplicate/out-of-order markers, command/package mismatches, initial wrapper/stderr/test-name lookalikes, chunk/ANSI/CRLF/EOF splits, fake private directory suffixes, long intervening output, finite monotonic fake clocks, missing initialization, pre-cleanup freezing and late drain/cleanup events. ## T012 bounded completed-test timing design - 2026-10-18 UTC; proposed, not implemented

### Evidence, question and exact scope

Latest CI reached the unchanged outer ceiling at 900111 ms; the last paired `test` announcement was at 11175 ms, leaving an observed interval of 888936 ms. This establishes neither a named slow/failing test nor a causal explanation. The owner now requests a design only, without another campaign or any guard/limit/timeout change.

Propose a **memory-only diagnostic variant of the existing bounded capturer**, using Vitest's built-in verbose reporter, not another P profiler/controller/reporter/plugin. Leave the qualified CI source (`55FF11AD6C1706FF145595FF4F295C14EA79482B414CE53188768EAEB830281A`) immutable. Proposed repository edits remain only this design and `docs/runbooks/phase-q-acceptance.md`; no package/lockfile, Vitest configuration, test/runtime source, Harness catalog/validator/pin, normal state or sibling change. Do not modify or execute `scripts/profile-maintenance.ts` or enable any of its profiling flags.

Installed evidence: Vitest **4.1.11**; `vitest.config.ts` retains `maxWorkers: 2`, existing include/environment/setup and default per-test behavior. Installed `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/index.UpGiHP7g.js`, `VerboseReporter.onTestCaseResult`, emits the module, optional line/column, private title and reported duration at result time. Its inherited non-TTY summary does not provide live test starts, and its module printer is empty. Local installed code is authoritative for parsing; the [official reporter guide](https://vitest.dev/guide/reporters#verbose-reporter) corroborates completed-case and optional-location behavior, not version-specific implementation details.

If separately authorized after synthetic qualification, the **one proposed diagnostic command** is:

```text
pnpm test --reporter=verbose --includeTaskLocation
```

This is explicitly a diagnostic invocation with two reporting/location flags, **not the original `pnpm test` catalog entry, `pnpm ci:check`, or an acceptance substitute**. Native arguments are fixed to the existing pnpm entry, `test`, `--reporter=verbose`, `--includeTaskLocation`; no arbitrary options/paths, file/name filter, shard, bail, retry, worker/pool/sequencer/isolation override, custom loader, coverage, CPU/heap/FS tracing or output file. Keep the same credential-stripped environment/PATHEXT, TEMP/TMP and CI value. No global environment mutation, installation or dependency-resolution probe is proposed.

### Passive parser, privacy and fixed retention

1. Consume only complete stdout LF lines **after** unchanged raw strict-UTF-8 stream/line checks; normalize existing SGR and CRLF only afterward. Admit the exact locally inspected verbose result shape with a repository-listed test path, source line/column, supported status and anchored numeric duration/suffix. Match/discard the title as untrusted transient text; never save a title, parameter value, assertion, error body, absolute path or transcript. Stderr, annotations, summaries, console lookalikes, malformed/partial rows and unknown rendering cannot establish a valid completion observation. Child text is forgeable, so even a valid row remains reporter-shaped evidence, not execution authentication.
2. Pin a deterministic public test-file manifest from the fresh source baseline: currently **151 test files**, maximum relative path length **59**, under the existing cli/dashboard/lib/runner/scripts roots. Assign stable numeric file IDs in sorted order; retain the exact manifest identity and mapping in memory. Reject traversal, absolute/foreign paths, changed manifest/version/rendering and unknown IDs rather than scanning an output-supplied path or silently extending capacity. This manifest restricts observation, never test selection. Resolve only retained IDs to reviewed public source locations in the final documentary evidence.
3. Preallocate one numeric aggregate per admitted file, **three** ranked completion rows, **two** derived file-sum rows, one last-completion row, one first-failed-completion location and fixed counters/reason fields. No growing per-test map/list, task-name dictionary, event tail or raw-output archive. Rank by reported duration descending, then file ID/location/report ordinal; file sums by duration descending then file ID. Same-line parameterized/retried/repeated results remain separate **completion reports**, not invented unique tests or exclusive named parameter identities.
4. Each retained completion carries only numeric file ID, line/column, parent-receipt ordinal, reported duration and bounded status. Per-file aggregates record observed report count and summed reported duration, with explicit omitted/undated/unsupported counters. Missing duration is **unknown**, not zero. Validate nonnegative finite integer fields, supported suffixes, safe addition and monotonic parent clock; overflow, unsupported/inconsistent data or capacity drift makes affected attribution unavailable/partial, not native success or a new early-termination condition. Do not claim global uniqueness/deduplication from file/line alone.
5. Use only the existing supervisor stopwatch for parent receipt/last-completion silence intervals; no new timer, sampling or wait. Freeze the compact snapshot after capturing the original supervision reason and before termination/drain, or at confirmed native completion. Later output, cleanup, clock changes and caller mutations cannot rewrite it. Clear mutable state in finalization.
6. Replace the diagnostic-only projection's irrelevant CI-stage metadata with one `vitest_observation` object; **do not append a second unlimited record/channel**. Keep the existing normal/fallback result authority and sanitized diagnostic handling. Compact numeric IDs bound the serialized payload: top three completion reports, top two file sums, first failure location, last completion and counters/coverage must fit inside the existing **1024-byte metadata / 2048-byte whole-record** bounds, including diagnostic/fallback worst cases. Manifest, aggregates and unfinished stream text count toward the existing retained-payload budget; there is no additional allowance. Synthetic failure to fit means STOP, not automatic truncation, a raised cap or a broader design.

### Interpretation and unchanged supervision

The output answers **which observed completed reports were slowest** and **which observed files accumulated the most reported test duration**. Durations are framework-reported rounded milliseconds, not CPU time, exact callback-only cost, critical path or additive wall time: concurrent test/worker intervals overlap. File sums are not complete module runtime and cannot explain transform/import/collection/environment, unreported hooks, pending subprocess cleanup or the whole timeout. Parameterized cases may share a location. Last completed is not currently running; a long silence is an observation gap, not proof a particular test hung. No missing row, skipped case or absent result is assigned zero cost or PASS. A new diagnostic cannot recover the previous discarded output and its reporting overhead may affect timing.

Keep the **900-second whole-command predicate and origin**, **65536-byte retained-stream**, **8192-byte raw-line**, **1024-byte message**, **2048-byte terminal** bounds, **1024-character reads**, **5-second exit wait**, **3-second drain**, Kill(true), pending-read loop, completion predicates and principal/stream checks unchanged. No new runtime bail/failure-trigger or per-stage/per-test allowance is proposed: an observed failed-case location is retained, but native exit and the existing supervisor still determine the terminal result. Preserve its first reason even when metadata is partial. Native nonzero/unknown ends the authorized sequence without another command. Cleanup uncertainty remains explicit; never delete guessed roots.

| --- | --- |

| `last` | `[file_id, line, column, completion_report_ordinal, parent_receipt_ms]`, or null |
| `first_failed` | `[file_id, line, column]`, or null |

Status codes are `p` passed-shaped, `f` failed-shaped, `s` skipped-shaped and `t` todo-shaped. `reports`, `timed`, `undated`, `unsupported` and `omitted` describe observed reports, not authoritative final test totals; `omitted` counts duration-bearing rows outside the three retained slots. Per-file sums include only known reported durations; undated/unsupported coverage must remain visible. A valid banner/row remains forgeable child output. Its retained passed-shaped row was 67326 ms. | The intentionally running workflow must not be made to reconcile by a generic completion helper. |

`runTask.finish()` persists `lifecycle: finished` before its remaining return/finally work. `TaskOrchestrator.launch()` subsequently clears the heartbeat, removes the active attempt, releases ownership and calls workflow reconciliation in its promise `finally`. Therefore **task completion is not the same checkpoint as workflow reconciliation**. The immediate post-wait workflow assertions at 241 expose a real ordering assumption. This is a repairable synchronization risk, **not a proven explanation of the captured failure**.

In `correctionFixture.advance()`, the pre-dispatch `detail().stages.at(-1)` builds the complete public projection merely to select one queued stage. `createWorkflowService.detail()` reads/validates each stage task and computes its budget readiness. For the inspected seven-stage paths, those seven preliminary projections each traverse 1 through 7 tasks: **28 task-read/readiness pairs per scenario**, before other unchanged product and assertion reads. This is static call-path fan-out, not measured milliseconds or evidence that removing it alone will put CI below 900 seconds. The three retained long scenarios together contain 21 such preliminary DTO calls / 84 pairs. Full post-step projections remain necessary.

`discoverProjects()` is only a depth-one filesystem scan; it does not invoke Git. No expensive external discovery/provider call was established here, so replacing policy/discovery with fakes is not part of this repair. Real Git, writer worktrees, verification and source/current-candidate checks remain covered.

Do not modify `scripts/profile-maintenance.test.ts`: its historical-prefix hash and literal composition checks remain intact.
